kaṟpu peṇṇaik kaṭṭuppaṭuttiṟṟēyaṉṟi āṇaik kaṭṭuppaṭutta villai. cila camayaṅkaḷil peṇkaḷaiyum kaṭṭuppaṭuttavillai. maṇañ ceyta oruvaṉ āṇtaṉmai illātavaṉāyiruntāl (Impotent) ava ṉuṭaiya maṉaivi kaṇavaṉiṉ viruppōṭu avaṉuṭaiya cakōtaraṉai allatu neruṅkiya naṇpaṉaic cēralām eṉa ulūtar kūṟiyuḷḷār. āṇ, āṇ taṉmai uṭaiyavaṉāyirunta pōtu peṇ kaṟpāyirukka vēṇṭu meṉṟu koḷḷappaṭṭatu. āṇ campantappaṭṭavaḷavil avaṉ ivvā ṟirukka vēṇṭiyatillai; piṟamātaraic cērtal avaṉ vallamaikkuṭ paṭṭiruntatu. uyarnta kuṭumpaṅkaḷil avvāṟiruttal avaciyam eṉavum karutappaṭṭatu. mattiya kālaṅkaḷil parattaiyar viṭutikaḷ nakara vāḻkkaikku iṉṟiyamaiyātaṉaveṉṟum karutappaṭṭaṉa; tamatu maṉaiviyarallāta peṇkaḷaic cērtal oḻukkakkēṭu eṉṟu karutappaṭavillai. kōyiṟ kurumār māttiramallar vicuppāṇṭavarkaḷum parattaiyar viṭutikaḷil vari taṇṭiṉārkaḷ. urōmil pōppu parattaiyarkaḷiṭamiruntu vari taṇṭiṉār. pattām nūṟṟāṇṭil pōppiṉ kāriyālayattil 20,000 ṭakkaṭ (Dueat) ivvakaiyil variyākak kiṭaittataṟkup paṟṟuc cīṭṭukaḷ kāṇap paṭukiṉṟaṉa. jermaṉiyiluḷḷa mēyiṉ eṉṉumiṭattil ‘āṭci vicuppu’ ivvakai vari taṇṭiṉār. potup peṇkaḷ, parattaiyarōṭu pōṭṭiyiṭu vatāl tamatu varuvāy kuṟaintataippaṟṟi avar kuṟippiṭṭuḷḷār. srās pēkkiṉ vicuppu parattaiyar viṭuti oṉṟait toṭakkiṉār.

cila vakuppiṉariṭaiyē vilaimātar mika matippup peṟṟaṉar. avarkaḷ maṉaiviyarilum mēlāṉavarkaḷākak koḷḷappaṭṭaṉar. apicīṉiyāvilē parattaiyar aracaṉ araṇmaṉaiyil mika matippuk kuriyavarkaḷāyiruntaṉar. oru nakar allatu mākāṇattiṉar āṭci avar poṟuppil viṭappaṭṭatu. araciyaraip pōl uṭuttu avarkaḷ tamatu mattiyil iruppataip pārttu muṟaimaiyāṉa maṉaiviyar perumai koṇṭaṉar.

tantaiyaippaṟṟi mukkiyamiṉmai
maṉaiviyai viruntāḷikaḷukku iraval koṭuttal, tirumaṇamāṉa peṇṇaik kaṇavaṉallāta piṟar mutalmutal cērutal pōṉṟavaikaḷuk kum maṉaivi mūlam piṟakkum piḷḷaikaḷukkum toṭarpu irukkavillai. kaṇavar, piṟa āṭavar mūlam tamatu maṉaiviyar, piḷḷaikaḷaip peṟa aṉumatikkum nikaḻccikaḷ pala uṇṭu. avvāṟu piṟakkum piḷḷaikaḷaik kaṇavaṉ taṉatu piḷḷaikaḷākavē pāvikkiṉṟāṉ. eskimōvar tam maṉaiviyar tam matakuru mūlam piḷḷaikaḷaip peṟuvatai virumpiṉārkaḷ. mēlāṉa cantatiyaip peṟa virumpum arāpiyaṉ oruvaṉ maṉaivi pūppu aṭaintatum iṉṉoruvaṉait taṉatu maṉaiviyōṭu cērntirukkum paṭi viṭuvāṉ. avaḷ viruntāḷi mūlam karuvaṭaintāḷ eṉṟu aṟiyu muṉ kaṇavaṉ avaḷait tīṇṭamāṭṭāṉ. peṇ, piḷḷai illātavaḷāṉa pōtu nākarika kālattilum ivvakaic ceyalkaḷ nikaḻntaṉa.

paḻaiya yūtariṉ tirumaṇac caṭṭapaṭi iṟantupōṉa tamaiyaṉiṉ maṉaiviyaic cērumpaṭi oruvaṉ aṉumatikkappaṭṭāṉ. av vakaiyiṟ piṟakkum piḷḷai iṟantavaṉiṉ piḷḷaiyākak karutappaṭṭatu. kapīr makka ḷuḷ tantaiyiṉ maṉaivi avaṉ maraṇattukkuppiṉ avaḷ makaṉukkuc contamāṉāḷ. makaṉ tantaiyiṉ maṉaiviyait tīṇṭa māṭṭāṉ. avaṉ avaḷai iṉṉoruvaṉōṭu irukkumpaṭi cilakālam aṉumatittāṉ. avaḷ vayiṟṟil piṟakkum piḷḷai tantai mūlam vanta cottākak karutap paṭṭatu. intiyāvil niyōka maṇattiṉāl, piḷḷaiyillātavaṉiṉ maṉaivi kaṇavaṉ irukkumpōtē avaṉuṭaiya cakōtaraṉ mūlam allatu vēṟu uṟaviṉar mūlam piḷḷaikaḷaip peṟṟāḷ; piḷḷaikaḷ kaṇavaṉiṉ piḷḷaikaḷākak karutappaṭṭaṉa. ōceṭis (Ossetes) makkaḷiṭaiyē oruvaṉ taṉatu āṟu vayatu makaṉukku vaḷarnta peṇṇaik kaliyāṇam kaṭṭi vaittāṉ. kaṇavaṉiṉ tantai mūlam avaḷ vayiṟṟil piṟakkum piḷḷaikaḷ kaṇavaṉiṉ piḷḷaikaḷākak karutappaṭṭaṉa. teṉṉintiya piṟpōkkāṉa makkaḷ cilariṭaiyum ivvaḻakkam kāṇappaṭu vatait teṉṉintiya kulaṅkaḷum kuṭikaḷum eṉṉum nūlil tēsraṉ kāṭṭiyuḷḷār.

kaṟpiṉ ciṉṉamākiya nāṇam
nāṇam iyaṟkaiyāṉa kuṇamaṉṟu. atu piṟkālattil tōṉṟi vaḷarntatu. cila nāṭukaḷil āṇ peṇ cērkkai veḷippaṭaiyāka nikaḻkiṉṟatu. teṉ kaṭal tīvukaḷ, tākiti pōṉṟa paṟpala iṭaṅkaḷil itu potu nikaḻcci. cila piṟpōkkāṉa makkaḷuḷ āṇkaḷum peṇkaḷum uṭaiyiṉṟic celkiṉṟaṉar. pakkāri (Bakaire) makkaḷaik kuṟittu sṭiṉeṉ (Steinen) kūṟiyiruppatu varumāṟu: “namatu ūravarkaḷukku uṭampil maṟaivāṉa iṭameṉpatu kiṭaiyātu. maṟaivāṉa uṟuppu eṉak kūṟuvatē mūṭattaṉam eṉa avarkaḷ kūṟukiṟārkaḷ. oru āṇaiyō peṇṇaiyō avarkaḷiṉ piḷḷaiyaik kāṭṭi avar yār eṉak kēṭṭāl avarkaḷ tamatu irakaciya uṟuppukaḷait toṭṭukkāṭṭi avarkaḷ avaikaḷiliruntu tōṉṟiyavarkaḷ eṉak kūṟuvar.” avarkaḷ maṟṟavarkaḷukku muṉ niṉṟu uṇṇa nāṇukiṉṟaṉar. maṟṟavarkaḷ kāṇāmal uṇpatu naṟpaḻakkameṉa avarkaḷāl koḷḷappaṭukiṉṟatu. mukam, kāl, mayir, pātam, vayiṟu eṉpavaikaḷil ovvoṉṟaik kāṭṭa nāṇum makkaḷum kāṇappaṭukiṉṟārkaḷ. mukamatiyap peṇkaḷ mukattaik kāṭṭamāṭṭārkaḷ. pala makkaḷiṭaiyē makaṉ paṉṉiraṇṭu vayatāṉa piṉ tāy avaṉaip pārkka aṉumatikkappaṭamāṭṭāḷ. ekiptilē velakīṉ peṇkaḷ āṭavariṉ mattiyil tamatu uṭaiyai urintu uṭuppil nāṇa māṭṭārkaḷ. āṉāl, tamatu mukattai maṟṟavarkaḷ pārkkumpaṭi viṭamāṭ ṭārkaḷ. hoṟṟaṉ rōvar eṉṉum āppirikkap peṇkaḷ. yūtap peṇkaḷaip pōlat talaikku mēl oru tuṇiyaip pōrttiruppārkaḷ avarkaḷ tamatu mayirai maṟṟavarkaḷ pārkkumpaṭi orupōtum viṭamāṭ ṭārkaḷ. peluttit tīvil cāmā eṉṉum iṭattup peṇkaḷ tamatu irakiya uṟuppukkaḷai veḷiyē kāṭṭuvatil nāṇamaṭaiya māṭṭārkaḷ; koppūḻai veḷiyē kāṭṭuvatil nāṇamaṭaivar. cīṉap peṇkaḷ tamatu pātattai veḷiyē kāṭṭa nāṇuvar. pātattaik kuṟittup pēcuvatē virumpattakāta ceyalākum. ūkaṇṭāvil oruvaṉ taṉatu kāliṉ ciṟiya pakuti tāṉum veḷiyē teriyumpaṭi aracaṉukku etiril niṉṟāl avaṉ kolait taṇṭaṉai aṭaivāṉ; āṉāl, avaṉaic cēvikkum makaḷir muṟṟum uṭaiyiṉṟi niṉṟaṉar. mattiya āppirikkāvil pala kūṭṭattiṉa riṭaiyē peṇkaḷ marakkiḷai oṉṟai aṇivar. atu muṟpakkattai maṟaippataṟkup patil piṟpakkattai maṟaittatu. immarakkiḷai taṟceya lākat tavaṟi viṭṭāl avarkaḷ peritum nāṇamaṭaivar.

amerikka makkaṭ kūṭṭattiṉar palar aṉṉiyarukku muṉ uṭalil niṟaṅkaḷ pūcāmal cellamāṭṭārkaḷ. āṭaiyillāmal tiriyum makkaḷukku āṭai uṭuppatu tiṭukkāṭṭam viḷaivippatāyirukkiṉṟatu. mukammatiyap peṇkaḷ, mukattai maṟaikkāta peṇkaḷait tāḻntavarākak karutuvar. uṭalai maṟaiyāmal viṭuvatāl tōṉṟum nāṇam uṭai aṇivatālum alaṅkāram ceyvatālum uṇṭāṉateṉat terikiṉṟatu. nāṇattiṉāl uṭai tōṉṟavillai. uṭaiyiṉāl nāṇam tōṉṟiṟṟu. uṭaiyiṉṟi iruntu veyiṟ kāyum (Sun bath) peṇkaḷ mutalil nimirntu pārkka nāṇuvārkaḷ. paḻakkam uṇṭāṉa piṉpu avarkaḷ maṟṟavarkaḷōṭu naṉṟākak kalantu pēcuvārkaḷ.

kaṟpu matippukkuriyatāṉa pōtu atu uṭal campantamāṉa nāṇat tōṭu iṇaittup palappaṭuttappaṭṭatu. ciṟuvar iḷamai mutal tamatu cila uṟuppukaḷaik kuṟittu nāṇam aṭaiyumpaṭi paḻakkappaṭukiṉṟaṉar. ic caṭṭaṅkaḷai mīṟukiṉṟavarkaḷait taṇṭippatāka araciṉarum payamuṟuttu kiṉṟaṉar. itaṉāl iṉṉum nāṇam valiyuṟuttappaṭukiṉṟatu.

mattiya kālattil jermaṉiyil kuṭicaikaḷil vāḻpavarkaḷ nirvāṇamākap paṭukkaikkuc ceṉṟārkaḷ. ciṟiya vīṭukaḷiluḷḷavarkaḷ orē paṭukkaiyil paṭuttārkaḷ. talaikkuk kaṭṭik koḷḷum nāṭāmāt tiram irākkāla uṭaiyāka viruntatu. ṭeṉmārkkil iravil muṟṟum uṭaiyiṉṟi paṭukkaikkuc cellum vaḻakkam 17ām nūṟṟāṇṭu varai yil iruntatu. 1658il ṭeṉmārkkukkuc ceṉṟa pōlīs irāṇuva atikāri ṭeṉmārkkiluḷḷavarkaḷ eppaṭip paṭukkaikkuc ceṉṟārkaḷ eṉṟu kūṟiyuḷḷār. 13ām nūṟṟāṇṭiṉ ōviyaṅkaḷ eppaṭi āṭavarum makaḷirum kuḷittārkaḷ eṉpataik kāṭṭukiṉṟaṉa. uṭaiyillāta āṇum peṇṇum orē kālattil orē iṭattil niṉṟu kuḷittārkaḷ. ōviyam oṉṟil āṭavaṉ araiyil tuṇikaṭṭi niṟkiṉṟāṉ. peṇ uṭaiyiṉṟik kaḻuttil mālai aṇintu koṇṭu talaiyai maṟaittuk koṇṭu niṟkiṉṟāḷ. mattiyakāla uroṭṭic curuḷkaḷ āṇ peṇkaḷiṉ irakaciya uṟuppukkaḷiṉ vaṭivākac ceyyappaṭṭaṉa. avvuṟuppukaḷiṉ peyar kaḷālēyē attiṉpaṇṭaṅkaḷ aṟiyappaṭṭaṉa. mariyātaiyuḷḷavarkaḷ, paricuttam niṟaintavarkaḷ, kurumār, kaṉṉippeṇkaḷ, tantaimār, tāy mārkaḷiṉ vāykaḷāl appeyarkaḷ uccarikkappaṭṭaṉa. avvāṟu ceyvatil taṭaiyātum eḻavillai.

tantaiyiṉ matippu
celvam tiraḷat toṭaṅkiyapōtu oruvaṉ taṉatu celvattait taṉatu irattat toṭarpuḷḷa piḷḷaikaḷukku viṭa virumpiṉāṉ. ākavē, tirumaṇam mukkiya kaṭamaiyāka viruntatu. paḻaiya pōkkil piḷḷai kaḷai vaḷarppatu ilakuvāyiruntatu. nākarikam ēṟa ēṟa itu kaṭiṉamāyiṟṟu. kaṇavaṉ piḷḷaikaḷukkut tāṉē poṟuppu eṉa aṟintu avarkaḷiṉ cumaiyait tāṉē taṉatu tōḷkaḷil ēṟṟāṉ. iccumaiyai iṉṉoru vaṉ taṉatu tōḷiṉmēl cumattiṉāl atu ēyppākak karutappaṭṭatu. ākavē avaṉ kōpam aṭaintāṉ.

kuṭumpa kālam
kuṭumpa kālattil peṇ āṭavaṉukkuk kīḻppaṭṭu naṭakka vēṇṭi yavaḷ eṉak karutappaṭṭāḷ. akkālattil avaḷ taṉippaṭṭavaḷākak karutappaṭavillai. peṇ āṭavaṉiṉ vīṭṭaip pārppavaḷākavum, avaṉuṭaiya piḷḷaikaḷaip peṟupavaḷākavum iruntāḷ. āṭavaṉ avaḷuṭaiya talaivaṉāka iruntāṉ. tirumaṇattiṉāl peṇ tantai yiṉ kavaṉippiliruntu kaṇavaṉiṉ kīḻppaṭivatāka viratam ceyyavēṇṭum. kaṇavaṉuṭaiya eṇṇattiṉpaṭi avaḷ naṭakka vēṇṭum. ākavē kaṇava ṉuṭaiya eṇṇappaṭi avaḷuṭaiya kuṇaṅkaḷ māṟupaṭṭaṉa. avaḷ mika eḷitil kīḻppaṭivuḷḷavaḷākum poruṭṭu, avaḷ aṉupavamillāta vaḷāyum, kapaṭamaṟṟa kaṟpuṭaiyavaḷāyum, tuṇivuṭaiyavaḷāyum, acca muḷḷavaḷāyum iruttal vēṇṭum. avaḷuṭaiya vāḻkkai āṭavaṉiṉ vāḻkkaiyuḷ aṭaṅkiyiruttal vēṇṭum. avaḷ avaṉai makiḻvikka vēṇṭumāṉāl avaḷ mēṟkūṟiya peṇṇukkuriya kuṇaṅkaḷuṭaiya vaḷāyiruttal vēṇṭum.
peṇkaḷ mutalāḷittaṉattiṉ kīḻ vāḻvatāl cila tīmaikaḷum tōṉṟiṉa. vēlaikkārarākkappaṭṭa peṇkaḷ kīḻmai aṭaintārkaḷ. avarkaḷiṭattil āṭavariṉ aṭakku muṟaikku māṟāṉa kuṇaṅkaḷum eḻuntaṉa. āṭavaṉiṭattil atikāramiruntamaiyāl avaḷ, tantiram, cūḻcci, poy mutaliya kuṇaṅkaḷ uṭaiyavaḷāṉāḷ. avaḷ periya karumaṅkaḷil kalantu koḷḷavillai. ākavē avaḷ tīmai purital, kuḻappam ceytal, atikam pēcutal, aḻutal pōṉṟa kuṇaṅkaḷ uṭaiya vaḷāṉāḷ. avaḷ aṉupavamum, kapaṭamum illāmal iruntatāl avaḷ āṭavaṉōṭu paḻakkamillātavaḷāyumiruntāḷ. ivvakaiyāṉa maṉap piḷavupaṭṭa nilaiyāṉatu orutāra maṇattiṉāl uṇṭāyiṟṟu. avarkaḷ oruvarai oruvar viḷaṅkātavarkaḷāyum, vevvēṟu viruppuṭaiyavar kaḷāyum kaliyāṇattuḷ iruntārkaḷ. itu irakaciyamāṉa pōrāṭṭattai ottiruntatu. ip pōrāṭṭattil āṭavaṉ eppoḻutum veṟṟiyāḷaṉā yirukkavillai. peṇ, poruḷ campantamāṉa cutantiramillātavaḷāṉa paṭiyāl maṉitaṉ avaḷai vāḻnāḷ muḻuvatum vaittuk kāppāṟṟa vēṇṭiyavaṉāṉāṉ.

kuṭumpattil peṇ vāḻa vēṇṭiya muṟaiyai maṉu kūṟukiṉṟār; peṇṇē avamāṉattukkuk kāraṇam; avaḷē pakaikkum ētu; peṇ taṉatu vāḻnāḷ muḻuvatum kaṇavaṉukkup paṇiviṭai ceyvatu māttiramallāmal avaṉ iṟanta piṉpum avaṉukku vicuvācamāyirukka vēṇṭum; kāraṇamiṉṟi avaṉ avaḷai ēyttālum, avaṉ iṉṉoru peṇṇōṭu naṭpāyiruntālum avaḷ avaṉait teyvam pōl mariyātai ceyya vēṇṭum. avaḷ avaṉai vāḻnāḷilō maraṇattiṉ piṉpō veṟukkumpaṭi ceytal kūṭātu. peṇ kaṇavaṉiṉ maraṇattiṉ piṉ avaṉukku vicuvācamāyirukkum poruṭṭuc cati eṉṉum vaḻak kattaip pirāmaṇarkaḷ uṇṭu paṇṇi uyirōṭu irukkum vitavaiyaik kaṇavaṉōṭu kaṭṭaiyil kiṭattik koḷuttiṉārkaḷ.

ilakkiya kālattil kirīcil allatu urōmil peṇkaḷiṉ nilai itaṟku mēlāka iruntateṉak kūṟa muṭiyavillai. arisṭāṭṭil eṉṉum kirēkka ñāṉiyār, peṇ iyaṟkaiyiṉ māṟupāṭu eṉṟum, koṭiya vilaṅkukaḷuḷ mutaṉmaiyāṉavaḷ eṉṟum kūṟiṉār. oruvaṉ āyiram peṇkaḷilum pārkka mēlāṉavaṉ eṉa iyupiriṭis (Eupirides) kūṟiṉār. piṟarāl naṉmai allatu tīmai kūṟa muṭiyātapaṭi vāḻpavaḷē nallavaḷ eṉat tukiṭiṭīs kūṟiṉār. akkālap peṇkaḷ ciṟaiyilaṭaik kappaṭṭavarkaḷai pōla vāḻntārkaḷ. akkālap peṇkaḷ paṇivuḷḷa aṭimaikaḷākak karutappaṭṭaṉar. kuṭumpa kālattil putalviyarilum pārkkap putalvar virumpappaṭṭaṉar. putalvaṉ viṭutalaiyuḷḷa vaṉāvāṉ; putalvi iṉṉoruvaṉukku aṭaṅki naṭappavaḷāvāḷ. kirēkkap pulavarkaḷ peṇkaḷaip paṟṟi varuṇittiruppatu mika mika viyap pākat tōṉṟukiṟatu. “tirumaṇa makiḻcci iraṇṭu muṟai māttiram aṟiyap paṭum. oṉṟu maṇamāṉa aṉṟu, maṟṟatu avaḷai iṭukāṭṭiṟ putaikkum pōtu” eṉa oru pulavar kūṟiyuḷḷār. “peṇ paṭukkaiyilum cavak kālai yilum nallavaḷ” eṉṉum mutumoḻi urōmil vaḻaṅkiṟṟu.

muṟkālattil tirumaṇam cumaiyuṭaiyatākavum tīmaiyuṭaiya tākavum karutappaṭṭatu; makiḻcciyuṭaiyatākak koḷḷappaṭavillai. tiruttamillāta peṇṇiṉ iṇaippiṉāl makiḻcci uṇṭāka māṭṭātu. ṭemāstiṉīs (Demosthenes) eṉpār atēṉiyarukkuk kūṟiyatu varumāṟu: “eṅkaḷukku iṉpam aḷikka vilaimātar irukkiṉṟaṉar; eṅkaḷ uṭalaik kavaṉikka vaippāṭṭikaḷ irukkiṉṟaṉar. muṟaiyāṉa piḷḷaikaḷaip peṟavum vīṭṭaip pārkkavum muṟaimaiyāṉa maṉaivi yar irukkiṉṟaṉar”. piḷāṭṭōvum itē karuttiṟ pēciyuḷḷār. “oruvaṉ kaliyāṇam eṉṉum vaḻakkattukkuṭpaṭṭup piḷḷaikaḷaip peṟuvatu tāṉākavaṉṟu, caṭṭattukkaṭaṅki” eṉpatu avar kūṟṟu. ki.mu. 132il oru mēl uttiyōkattaṉ, “nām maṉaivimāriṉṟi naṉṟākak kālaṅ kaḻikka vēṇṭumāṉāl ellōrum iccumaiyiliruntu viṭutalaiyaṭaiya vēṇṭum” eṉak kūṟiṉāṉ. ikkaruttē kuṭumpak kālattil iruntu vantatu. paḻaiya uruciyāvil peṇṇiṉ tantai cavukkiṉāl peṇṇai mella aṭittu, “nāṉ ituvaraiyum ceytu vanta vēlaiyai iṉi uṉ kaṇavaṉ ceyvāṉ” eṉa avaḷiṭam kūṟik kaṇavaṉiṭam cavukkaik koṭuttāṉ. ivvāṟu ceytal kaliyāṇak kiriyaiyil oru pakutiyāka iruntu vantatu. kurōciyāvil (Croatia) atu mutal peṇṇukkut talaivaṉ tāṉē eṉṟu kāṭṭum poruṭṭuk kaliyāṇattiṉ pōtu kaṇavaṉ peṇṇai aṭittāṉ.

kātalum maṇamum pirintu cellutal
kirēkkap peṇkaḷ antappuraṅkaḷil maṟiyaṟkāraraip pōl vāḻntārkaḷ. avarkaḷukkuk kalviyillai. kaṇavarallāta maṟṟavar kaḷōṭu paḻakātapaṭi avarkaḷ taṭukkappaṭṭārkaḷ. vēlaikkāri pōṉṟa vāḻkkai naṭatti vantamaiyāl avarkaḷ kaṇavarukkuk kātal aḷikkak kūṭiyavarkaḷāyirukkavillai. ākavē kaṇavar parattaiyar illaṅkaḷai nāṭic ceṉṟaṉar. parattaiyar avarkaḷ maṉaiviyaraippōl illāmal, aḻaku, tiruttam, ulaka aṟivu, kalaiviruppu mutaliya taṉmaikaḷ uṭaiyavarkaḷāyiruntaṉar.

“kurumār. cakkaravartti, pirapu, ‘naiṭ,’ pulavarkaḷ ellōrum ‘vīṉas’ teyvattiṉ āṭcikkuṭpaṭṭiruntaṉar. ‘naiṭ’ eṉṉum pirapukkaḷ tamatu maṉaiviyarukkuc cēvai purivatillai; maṇamāṉa peṇkaḷiṉ kātaliṉ poruṭṭuc cēvai purintaṉar. itaṉāl maṇamum kātalum pirikkappaṭṭiruntaṉa”.

11ām, 12ām nūṟṟāṇṭukaḷil kaṉṉi maṭaṅkaḷum kurumār maṭaṅkaḷum camīpaṅkaḷiliruntaṉa. pattikkum, paṭippukkum patil pala maṭaṅkil cōmpal, ācāṭapūtittaṉam, keṭṭa naṭattai mutaliyavai tōṉṟiṉa.

maṇam kuṭumpa campantamākaviruntatu. atu taṉippaṭṭa oruvaṉiṉ campantamuṭaiyatāyirukkavillai. kaṇavaṉ allatu maṉaiviyait terivatu peṟṟōriṉ kaṭamaiyākaviruntatu. peṇṇiṉ tantai perum pālum celvamuṭaiya kaṇavaṉait terintāṉ. kirīṉlāntil vitavaikaḷ kaṇavaṉiṉ maraṇakāla viruppattil kuṟippiṭappaṭṭavaṉai maṇañ ceytārkaḷ; avarkaḷ tām virumpiyapaṭi maṇam ceyyavillai. cīṉā vil maṇamakaṉ kaliyāṇattiṉ aṉṟutāṉ peṇṇaip pārppāṉ.

kātal viṭutalai
viḻākkaḷ, nāṭṭiyaṅkaḷ, nīrāṭum vaḻakkaṅkaḷ, viyapicāra viṭutikaḷ, irakaciyamāṉa naṭippukkaḷ pōṉṟa nikaḻccikaḷil paḻam pōk kāṉa kātal viṭutalai uṇṭāyiruntatu. āṇ peṇ cērkkai toṭar pāṉa kaṭṭukkaḷ nīkkappaṭṭiruntaṉa.

āṟāvatu alakcāntariṉ pōppuc capaiyil kaṭṭaḻaku vāynta kaṭṭāṉa peṇkaḷum tēkakkaṭṭuḷḷa vēlaiyāṭkaḷum cērumpaṭi viṭappaṭṭārkaḷ. itaṉaic capaiyiluḷḷavarkaḷ ellōrum pārttu makiḻntu poḻutu pōkkiṉārkaḷ1. pirapukkaḷ kūṭṭaṅkaḷil oruva ṉuṭaiya maṉaivi orē kālattil iṉṉoruvaṉōṭu kātal campantappaṭ ṭiruntāl avaḷ kaṇavaṉ ataik kuṟittuk kavalai koḷḷavillai. akkūṭṭattiluḷḷa peṇkaḷ tamatu iccaikku nirampiya viruntu aḷittu vantārkaḷ. uruciya nāṭṭuc cakkaravarttiṉi taṉatu aṟaiyil epiravēcu eṉṟu aḻaikkappaṭum oru tātiyai amarttiyiruntāḷ. taṉakkut takuti yuḷḷavaṉ eṉa oruvaṉai avaḷ aṟintāl avaṉuṭaiya tiṟamaiyaip paḻakip pārttu aṟivikkumpaṭi avaṉ avaḷiṭam aṉuppappaṭṭāṉ2.

cīrtiruttattilum parattaimai, viyapicāram, tamatu kaṇavarallā tavarkaḷōṭu peṇkaḷ cērtal, tamatu maṉaiviyarallāta peṇkaḷōṭu āṇkaḷ cērtal pōṉṟa kīḻ nīrōṭṭaṅkaḷ irukkiṉṟaṉa.

peṇṇaippeṟum vaḻikaḷ
valimaiyāl piṭittu maṇattal, māṟṟu maṇam, koṭaimaṇam, cēvaimaṇam, kaṭamaimaṇam, poruḷmaṇam, kātalmaṇam eṉa maṇattai ēḻu pirivukaḷākap pirikkalām. kaṇavaṉiṉ cakōtari allatu uṟaviṉaril orutti peṇṇiṉ cakōtaraṉ allatu avaḷ uṟaviṉaruḷ oruvaṉai maṇattal māṟṟumaṇam eṉappaṭum. koṭaimaṇameṉpatu kaṇavaṉiṭa miruntu peṇṇiṉ tantai cila vekumatikaḷaip peṟṟuk koṇṭu peṇṇaik koṭuttal. poruḷmaṇa meṉpatu peṇṇiṉ tantai peṇṇukkuc cītaṉamaḷittal. kaṭamai maṇameṉpatu cakōtaraṉiṉ vitavaiyai maṇattal allatu kuḻantaiyil maṇam pēci muṭivu ceyyap paṭṭiruntamaiyiṉ maṇattal pōṉṟavai. muṟkūṟiyavai oṉṟaiyum nōkkātu kātal kāraṇamāka maṇattal kātal maṇam eṉappaṭum.

maṇameṉpatu toṭakkattil peṇṇai aṭimaippaṭuttuvatā yiruntatu. itu peṇṇai valimaiyāṟ piṭittu uṟuttappaṭuvatu. maṉitaṉiṉ kīḻ nilaiyil avaḷ ellā vēlaiyāḷarilum pārkka mēlāka matikkappaṭṭāḷ. akkālattil vāṇikattālum paṇattālum celvam uṇṭākavillai. ākavē, oruvaṉukkup peṇ oruttiyaip piṭittuc contamākkuvatu iyalpāka iruntu vantatu. peṇ cērkkai campantamāṉa maṉitaṉiṉ iyaṟkai uṇarcci avaṉ kūṭṭattukku veḷiyilēyē ceṉṟatu. ākavē avaṉ peṇ oruttiyaip peṟa virumpiṉāl avaṉ avaḷai ayaṟ kūṭṭattiliruntu valimaiyāl piṭittāṉ. muṟkālattil ayalē vāḻum makkaṭ kūṭṭattiṉar oruvaruṭaṉ oruvar pakaimai koṇṭiruntaṉar. ākavē koḷḷaiyiṉ poruṭṭum pōrkaḷ nikaḻntaṉa. peṇ aṭimai mika vilai ēṟap peṟṟavaḷāyum maṉitaṉukku iṉṟiyamaiyātavaḷāyum iruntāḷ. muṟkālattil peṇkaḷaik kavarntu koṇṭu cellutal potu nikaḻccikaḷāyiruntaṉa. cāpaiṉs (Sabines) peṇkaḷaik kavartal, eliṉai tirāy aracaṉ kavartal, irāvaṇaṉ cītaiyaik kavartal pōṉṟa paḻaiya ceyalkaḷai varalāṟukaḷiṟ paṭikkiṉ ṟōm. ivaiyellām peṇkaḷai valimaiyāṟ piṭikkiṉṟa kāla nikaḻccikaḷiṉ niḻalkaḷēyākum. āstirēliya tasmēṉiyar ayaṟ kūṭ ṭaṅkaḷiliruntu peṇṇaik kavarntārkaḷ. ayal kūṭṭattiṉarōṭu avarkaḷ eppoḻutum pōr viḷaittaṉar. karips eṉṉum āppirikkar tām veṟṟi koṇṭa makkaḷiṉ āṇkaḷaik koṉṟu viṭṭup peṇ kaḷait tamakku maṉaiviyarākkuvataṟku aḻaittuc ceṉṟaṉar. kiṟittuva vētattil peṇkaḷaip piṭittalaip paṟṟiya ceyti kāṇappaṭukiṉṟatu. nītipatikaḷ eṉṉum puttakattil, peṉcamiṉiṉ putalvarkaḷ muntirikait tōṭṭattuḷ patuṅkiyiruntu naṭaṉamāṭik koṇṭirunta cillōviṉ putalviyarai maṇañ ceytaṟkākap piṭittārkaḷ. maṉuviṟ kūṟappaṭum eṇ vakai maṇaṅkaḷuḷ valimaiyāṟ peṇṇaip piṭikkum maṇam irākkatam eṉappaṭṭatu. urōmar jermaṉiyarkaḷiṭaiyil peṇkaḷai ivvāṟu piṭittal pukaḻccikkuriyatākaviruntatu. skāntiṉēviya paḻaiya pāṭal kaḷil peṇkaḷaik kavarum pōrkaḷ kūṟappaṭukiṉṟaṉa. pakaivaṉiṉ maṉaivi, makaḷ mutaliyōraik kavarntu maṇattai muṭivu ceyyum oḻukkamuṟai eṉṟu karutappaṭṭatu. kirēkka moḻiyil maṉaiviyaik kuṟikkum (Damar) eṉṉum col ‘aṭimai’ allatu pōril ciṟai yākkappaṭṭavaḷ eṉṉum poruḷpaṭum. teṉcilāviyariṭaiyē ‘peṇ piṭi maṇam’ in nūṟṟāṇṭiṉ toṭakkam varaiyil iruntatu. peṇ piṭi vaḻakkam niṉṟapōtum avaikaḷiṉ niḻalkaḷ nilaipeṟṟaṉa. spārṭ ṭāvil maṇamakaṉiṉ naṇpar peṇṇai valimaiyāl piṭittuk koṇṭu pōvatākiya maṇakkiriyai naṭattappaṭṭatu. puḷūṭō kālattil, peṇ tāyiṉ maṭiyil maṟaintiruppatākavum appoḻutu maṇamakaṉum avaṉuṭaiya naṇparum avaḷai iṭṭuc celvatākavum maṇakkiriyai naṭattappaṭṭatu. peṇ vācaṟ paṭiyait tāṉākat tāṇṭavillai; maṇa makaṉ avaḷait tūkki vāyiṟpaṭikku appālviṭṭāṉ. pala makkaḷiṭai yē peṇṇiṉ peṟṟōr avaḷ kaṇavaṉōṭu pēcutal ākātu eṉṉum caṭṭam iruntu varukiṉṟatu. itaṟkuk kāraṇam mēṟkūṟappaṭṭatē.

peṇ piṭiyiliruntu māṟṟuc caṭaṅku vaḷarcciyaṭaintatu. peṇ piṭiyiṉāl kūṭṭaṅkaḷiṭaiyē muṭivillāta pōrum pakaiyum uṇṭāyiruntaṉa. ākavē camātāṉa muṟaiyāṉa māṟṟuk kaliyāṇam naṉṟu eṉa avarkaḷ kaṇṭārkaḷ. peṇkaḷaik koṭuttu vāṅkuvatāl kūṭṭaṅkaḷiṭaiyē pakai taṇintatu. ākavē peṇ piṭiyiliruntu māṟṟuc caṭaṅku vaḷarcciyaṭaivatāyiṟṟu.

peṇṇiṉ tantaiyiṭam cēvai purintu peṇṇaip peṟuvatu ellā cātiyāriṭattum iyalpāka iruntu vantatu. yākkōppu, irāccel eṉpavaḷukkāka ēḻu āṇṭukaḷ cēvai purintār. peṇṇai vāṅku vataṟkup paṇamillāta maṇamakaṉ peṇṇiṉ tantaiyiṭam cēvai purital ellā nāṭukaḷilum vaḻakkāṟāka viruntatu. piyūci maṇamakaṉ peṇṇiṉ tantai tōṇi kaṭṭuvataṟku utavi purital vēṇṭum; puṣmaṉ maṇamakaṉ vēṭṭaiyāṭuvataṟku utavi purital vēṇṭum. amerikka makkaḷiṭaiyē maṇamakaṉ peṇṇiṉ tantai vīṭṭiṟ ceṉṟu vēlaiyāḷ pōla vēlai ceytal vēṇṭum. ivvakaic cēvai maṇam cērmaṉiyilum iruntatu. cēvai maṇamē payirc ceykai uḷḷa nāṭukaḷil tāyāṭciyāka māṟiṟṟu. kaṇavaṉ peṇṇiṉ kuṭumpattil ceṉṟu vāḻntāṉ. avaṉaic cūḻntu peṇṇiṉ uṟaviṉar vāḻntārkaḷ. cēvai maṇattukkup piṉ vilai koṭuttup peṇṇai vāṅkum maṇam tōṉṟiṟṟu.

peṇṇai vilaikoṭuttu vāṅkum maṇamum cītaṉa maṇamum
kaittoḻil, vāṇipam, āṭumāṭu mutaliyavaikaḷāl celvam vaḷarntatu. celvam āṭavarkaiyil iruntatu. mutalil vāṇikarum, mantai mēyppōrum āṭavarāyiruntaṉar. ākavē, avarkaḷ ap poḻutu peṇṇaip peṟum poruṭṭuc cēvai ceyya vēṇṭi yirukka villai. avarkaḷ peṇṇiṉ tantaikkup poruḷ koṭuttup peṇṇai vāṅkiṉārkaḷ. vilai koṭuttu vāṅkum maṇaṅkaḷōṭu cēvai maṇaṅkaḷum iruntu vantaṉa. cila nāṭukaḷil peṇṇiṉ tantaikkup poruḷ koṭāta maṇaṅkaḷ muṟaiyāṉa maṇaṅkaḷākak koḷḷappaṭa villai. amerikka vālipaṉoruvaṉ peṇṇoruttiyaik kātalittup peṇṇai vāṅkuvataṟku avaṉiṭam pōtiya poruḷ illātirukkumāṉāl avaḷuṭaiya tantai avaṉiṭamiruntu pāti vilaiyaip peṟṟu avaṉaip pāti maṇamuḷḷavaṉākkiṉāṉ. itaṉāl avaṉ appeṇṇait taṉatu kuṭicaikku aḻaittuc ceṉṟu avaḷait taṉatu aṭimaiyākka muṭiyāmal iruntatu; avaṉ avaḷ kuṭicaiyil taṅki avaḷukku aṭimaiyāṉāṉ.

celvam ēṟa ēṟac cēvai maṇaṅkaḷ vilai maṇaṅkaḷāyiṉa. vilai maṇattiṉāl peṇ, paṇṭaṅkaḷaip pōlak kaṇavaṉiṉ cottā ṉāḷ. vilai koṭuttu vāṅkuvatāl maṇam nilai uṭaiyatāyiṟṟu. kaṇavaṉ peṇṇiṉ kaliyāṇat toṭarpai orupōtum nīkka virumpa villai. avvāṟu ceytāl avaṉ peṇṇukku vilaiyākak koṭutta paṇattai iḻantu viṭuvāṉ. kāla nilaimai, poruḷātāra nilaimai kaḷukku ēṟṟavāṟu peṇṇiṉ vilai iruntatu. paṇam, kayiṟṟil ōṭu kaḷaik kōtta mālaikaḷ, kampaḷaṅkaḷ, kutiraikaḷ, āṭumāṭukaḷ, aṭimaikaḷ vakaiyil peṇkaḷiṉ vilai iruntatu. oṭṭaṉrōva peṇ oruttiyiṉ vilai oru erutu allatu oru pacu. kāruva peṇṇiṉ vilai mūṉṟu pacuvum oru āṭum. kapīr peṇṇiṉ vilai 6 mutal 30 māṭukaḷ; vilai peṇṇuṭaiya tantaiyiṉ perumaiyaip poṟuttiruntatu. poṅkōp peṇṇiṉ vilai 20 irāttal irumpu, 20 īṭṭi muṉai; vayatāṉavaḷāyiṉ malivākak kiṭaippāḷ. tokos peṇṇiṉ vilai 16 veḷḷiyum āṟu veḷḷi vilaiyuḷḷa paṇṭaṅkaḷum. kaliṉāp peṇṇiṉ vilai nāṟpatu mutal aṟupatu mārkku paṇam. kamrūṉ peṇṇiṉ vilai 6,000 ciliṉ; cātāraṇa peṇṇiṉ vilai 2,000 ciliṉ; aṭimaiyāyiṉ 800 ciliṉ. peṇkaḷ paṇamākavum payaṉpaṭuttappaṭṭār kaḷ. peruntokaip paṇam peṇkaḷ vakaiyākavum celuttappaṭṭatu.

alaintu tiriyum āciyap peṇkaḷuḷ kalamukakēṭ, tattārip peṇkaḷiṉ vilai mika atikam. oru kaliṉ peṇṇiṉ vilai 90 nāṉku vayatuṭaiya kutirai, 90 nālu vayatu āṭu, 90 nālu vayatu oṭṭai. tuṅku makkaḷuḷ oru peṇṇiṉ vilai 20 turuvamāṉ. vitavaiyāyiṉ malivākak kiṭaippāḷ. turkōmaṉ makkaḷuḷ oru peṇṇiṉ vilai 5 oṭṭakam; oru vitavaiyiṉ vilai 50 oṭṭakam. cīṉāvil oru peṇṇiṉ vilai 1,800 mārk varaiyil. vilai maṇam epirēyaruk kiṭaiyilum carmaṉiyaruk kiṭaiyilum iruntatu. hōciyā (Hosea) eṉpār peṇṇiṉ vilaiyāka 50 cekkal koṭuttār. kaliyāṇattaik kuṟikkum ēraṉreṉ eṉṉum carmaṉ collukku vāṅkutal allatu kūlikku viṭutal eṉpatu poruḷ.

cantai maṇam
pala cātiyiṉar maṇapparuvamaṭainta peṇkaḷaic cantaikkuk koṇṭu ceṉṟu vilai kūṟiviṟṟārkaḷ. ivvakai maṇam pāpilōṉil naṭaipeṟṟatai eratōtacu kuṟippiṭṭuḷḷār. avar kūṟiyiruppatu varu māṟu: “mecapetōmiyāvil āṇṭil oru muṟai maṇap paruvamuḷḷa peṇkaḷ ōr iṭattukkuk koṇṭu varappaṭukiṟārkaḷ. aṅku avarkaḷ vilai kūṟi ēlattil viṟkappaṭṭārkaḷ. viṟpaṉai mika aḻakāṉa peṇṇi liruntu ārampamāyiṟṟu. avaḷ vilaiyāṉatum avaḷukku aṭutta paṭiyiluḷḷa aḻaki viṟkappaṭṭāḷ. ivvāṟu viṟkappaṭum peṇkaḷai vāṅkiyavarkaḷ avarkaḷai maṇañceytu koṇṭārkaḷ. mika aḻakāṉa peṇṇai vāṅkuvataṟkup pāpilōṉiya vālipar pōṭṭiyiṭṭārkaḷ. maṇam muṭikka virumpiya potumakkaḷ aḻakillāta peṇkaḷai vāṅkiṉārkaḷ. avarkaḷukku oru tokaip paṇam koṭukkappaṭṭatu. aḻakāṉa peṇkaḷ vilaiyāṉavuṭaṉ aḻakillāta uṟuppuk kuṟai pāṭuṭaiya peṇkaḷ viṟkappaṭṭārkaḷ. avarkaḷaik kūṟumpōtu avar kaḷukkuk koṭukkum iṉām paṇamum cērttuk kūṟappaṭṭatu. ip paṇam aḻakāṉa peṇkaḷai viṟṟatiṉāl kiṭaittatu. iv vaḻakkam illīriyā vil vāḻum eṉeri makkaḷuṭaiyē toṭaṅkiyatu”. alakcāntar intiyā mītu paṭaiyeṭuttapōtu ivvakaic cantai vaṭakkē naṭaipeṟṟatākak kirēkka carittirakkārar eḻutiyuḷḷārkaḷ. arāpiyāvil acir (Asyr) vakuppiṉar maṇap paruvamaṭainta peṇkaḷai naṉṟāka uṭuttic cantai kaḷukkuk koṇṭu ceṉṟu aṅkumiṅkum ulāvumpaṭi viṭṭu avarkaḷai viṟṟārkaḷ. nākarika kālattil vāṅkum maṇam cītaṉa maṇamāka māṟiṟṟu. ōmar kālattil peṇṇiṉ tantai kaliyāṇa vekumatiyiloru pakutiyaip peṇṇukkuc cītaṉamākak koṭuttāṉ. vilai koṭuttu vāṅkum kaliyāṇam cākcaṉiyar, āṅkilō cākcaṉiyar, ṭāṉiyarkaḷuk kiṭaiyilum jermaṉ cātiyārkaḷukkiṭaiyilum kāṇappaṭṭatu. āṅkilō cākcaṉiyarukkiṭaiyilirunta iv vaḻakkam kāṉyūṭ eṉṉum aracaṉāl oḻikkappaṭṭatu. cīṉāvil iv vaḻakkam iṉṟum iruntu varukiṉṟatu.

vilai koṭuttu vāṅkappaṭṭa peṇ vilai koṭuttu vāṅkap paṭum poruḷkaḷ pōlāṉāḷ. makkaḷiṭaiyē tiruttamuṇṭāṉa pōtu peṇṇiṉ tantai itaṉai veṟuttāṉ. taṉatu putalviyar viyāpārap poruḷkaḷ pōla matikkappaṭuvatai avaṉ virumpavillai. uyarnta kuṭumpaṅkaḷiṭaiyē peṇkaḷai viṟkum vaḻakkam maṟaintatu; iṉṟēl vilaippaṇattil oru pakuti peṇṇukkuk koṭukkappaṭṭatu. itaṉāl peṇṇukkuk kaṇavaṉuṭaiya vīṭṭil cila urimaikaḷ uṇṭāyiṉa. kaṇavaṉiṉ vaippāṭṭikaḷaiviṭat taṉatu makaḷ uyarntavaḷākak karutap paṭum poruṭṭum peṇṇiṉ tantai cītaṉam aḷittāṉ. piṉpu vilai koṭuttup peṇṇai vāṅkutal anākarikattiṉ kuṟi eṉṟu karutap paṭṭatu. peṇṇukkuc cītaṉam koṭuttal tantaiyiṉ kaṭamaiyākak karutap paṭṭatu. cītaṉa maṇam vilai maṇattiliruntu vaḷarntatu. cītaṉam, paṇa mākavum paṇṭaṅkaḷākavum koṭukkappaṭṭatu. peṇṇukkuk kaṇavaṉait terital kuṭumpam karumamākavum peṟṟōriṉ kaṭamaiyākavum iruntatu. peṟṟōr maṇattukkut takutip poruḷeṉak koṇṭaṉar. maṇamakaṉum, maṇamakaḷum paṇattukku aṭuttapaṭiyil vaittuk karutappaṭṭaṉar. oruvaṉ virumpiyavāṟu maṇañ ceytalum aṅkumiṅkum kāṇappaṭukiṉ ṟatu. paḻaiya karuttukkaḷai mīṟuvatu kaṭumaiyāka virukkiṉṟatu. tiruttam vaḷarcciyaṭaivataṟkup pāṭalkaḷ mukkiyamāṉavai. pāṭalkaḷ vālipa muṭaiya peṇṇum āṇum oruvarai kātalippatākavum, poruḷātārat toṭarpāka mūttōr kuṟukkiṭuvatākavum ataṉāl iruvarum pala tuṉpaṅkaḷukkuḷḷāvatākavum cittarikkiṉṟaṉa.

kātal maṇam
cītaṉa maṇattiṉ piṉ iṉṉoru māṟṟam uṇṭāyiṟṟu. paṇattukkākac ceyyum maṇattaip potu uṇarcci kaṇṭikkiṉṟatu. vilai koṭuttup peṇṇai vāṅkum maṇam eppaṭi vilaṅkut taṉmai eṉak karutappaṭṭatō avvāṟē cītaṉa maṇamum karutappaṭalāyiṟṟu. cītaṉattukkāka maṇam ceyvatu ik kālattil uṇṭāṉataṉṟu. muṟkālat til itu irukkavillai eṉa niṉaittal kūṭātu. tirumaṇam kuṭumpak karumam eṉṟu karutappaṭum aḷavil itu naṭaipeṟavē ceyyum. “ēkkarukkum ēkkarukkum muṭikkum muṭikkum maṇam” eṉṉum mutu moḻi mēlnāṭukaḷil vaḻaṅkum. maṇattukku muṉṉāl kuṭumpattiṉa ruṭaiyavum uṟaviṉaruṭaiyavum poruḷātāra nilai kavaṉikkappaṭuvatu iyalpu. kātal taṉippaṭṭavarkaḷiṉ viruppattai uṟuttuvatu. peṇ kaḷiṭaiyē vēṟupāṭukaḷum kuṭumpattil pirivum uṇṭāṉapōtu taṉip paṭṭa kātal maṇaṅkaḷ vaḷarntaṉa. kātal poruḷātārattai nōkkuva tillai. paṇattukkākac ceyyappaṭum maṇaṅkaḷ kātal maṇaṅkaḷāka vaḷarntaṉa. peṇkaḷ poruḷātāra campantamāka aṭaiyum viṭutalaik kēṟpa ituvum vaḷarcciyuṟum.
iṉṟu ulakil kaittoḻilkaḷ perukiyuḷḷamaiyāl peṇ kaḷum āṇkaḷaippōl poruḷ campātikkiṉṟaṉar. kaittoḻilal lāta vēṟu tuṟaikaḷilum avarkaḷ āṭavarai oppa vēlai purintu ūtiyam peṟukiṉṟaṉar. ākavē, ituvaraiyum avarkaḷait taṭaippaṭutti yirunta poruḷātārap piṇippu viṭuvikkappaṭṭuḷḷatu. poruḷātārap piṇippē peṇkaḷai nīṇṭakālam āṭavariṉ aṭimaikaḷāka vait tiruntatu. peṇ poruḷ campantamākat taṉatu kālil niṟka muṭiyum pōtu avaḷ viṭutalaiyaṭaintuḷḷāḷ. avaḷ tāṉ virumpuvatupōl vāḻalām. avaḷukkum cātāraṇa kuṭumpattiluḷḷa peṇṇukkum kātal campantattil vēṟupāṭu uṇṭu. putu vakaiyāṉa peṇ taṉatu vēlaiyi ṉāl viṭutalaiyaṭaintāḷ. avaḷ āṭavaṉaip pōlac cama urimai aṭaikiṉṟāḷ.

ippoḻutu peṇṇiṉ cumai kuṟaivākkappaṭukiṉṟatu. muṟ kālattil āṭavaṉ kaṭṭaḷaiyiṭṭa vēlaikaḷaiyellām avaḷ ceytāḷ. avaḷ ivvulakattaip piḷḷaikaḷāl nirappiṉāḷ. avaḷ vāḻnāḷ karuppamāyiruttal, piḷḷaikaḷaip peṟutal, avarkaḷai vaḷarttal aḷavil iruntatu.

iṉṟaiya maṉitaṉ, vīṭṭaip pārkkum maṉaiviyaḷavil tiruptiyaṭaiyavillai; avaḷ taṉṉaippōla aṟivu viḷakkamuṭaiya vaḷāyiruppatil tiruppatiyaṭaikiṟāṉ. oru toḻil ceypavaṉ atē pōṉṟa toḻil purikiṉṟavaḷai maṇappatil makiḻcciyaṭaikiṉṟāṉ.

oḻukkat taḷarvu
ulaka makkaḷiṭaiyē āṇ peṇ toṭarpāṉa oḻukkaṅkaḷ palavāṟākaviruntaṉa. teṟku antamāṉ peṇkaḷiṭaiyē nāṇam mika vaḷarcciyaṭaintiruntatu. avarkaḷ tam muṉ pōrvaiyai peṇkaḷ muṉpākavum eṭukka nāṇiṉārkaḷ. āṉāl ayaṟ kūṭṭattiluḷḷa peṇkaḷ muṟṟum nirvāṇamākac celkiṉṟārkaḷ.

ittāliyil patiṉēḻām nūṟṟāṇṭiṉ muṭivil orutti iraṇṭu allatu mūṉṟu kātalarai vaittiruttal paṟṟi avaḷ kaṇavaṉ yātum vēṟu pāṭāka niṉaikkavillai. ivvakai vaḻakkam pirāṉcilē patiṉeṭ ṭām nūṟṟāṇṭil kāṇappaṭṭatu. cātāraṇa kālattil taṉ mārpaik kaṇavaṉ muṉ mūṭi maṟaikkum oru peṇ pāl (Ball) eṉṉum naṭaṉattil taṉatu muṉ puṟattiṉ mēṟpākattai ellārum pārkkumpaṭi tiṟantuviṭa nāṇamāṭṭāḷ. mēṟkut tēcaṅkaḷil ōviyap paḷḷikkūṭaṅkaḷil uṭal nilaiyaip pārttu ōviyam eḻutum poruṭṭup peṇkaḷ nirvāṇamāka niṟutti vaikkappaṭuvar. avarkaḷ niṟkum nērattukkut takkatāka maṇikku iṉṉateṉṟu kūli koṭukkappaṭukiṉṟatu. pala nāṭukaḷil pirapukkaḷ, allatu potumakkaḷ maṉaiviyaruḷ oruttiyai aracaṉ terinteṭut tāṉāyiṉ avarkaḷ peritum kaḷippaṭaintaṉar. oru cātiyāriṭaiyē kāṇappaṭum āṇ peṇ cērkkait toṭarpāṉa oḻukkaṅkaḷ iṉṉoru cātiyārukku veṟuppaḷikkalām. pala peṇkaḷai oruvaṉ maṇakkum nāṭṭiṉar oru peṇṇai oruvaṉ maṇappavaṉait tāḻntava ṉākak karutuvar. peṇ orutti pala kaṇavarai maṇakkum nāṭṭiṉar orutti oruvaṉai maṇappataip paṟṟi iḻivākak karutuvar. cakōtara cakōtari maṇamuṭaiya ekiptu pāracīka nāṭṭiṉar maṟṟaiya nāṭṭu vaḻakkaṅkaḷai veṟuppar.

paḻaiya makkaḷiṉ kātal
toṭakkattil kātal iyaṟkai uṇarcciyiṉ tūṇṭutalāka viruntatu. itil uyarnta paṇpukaḷ evaiyum irukkavillai. vilaṅku kaḷiṭattil kāṇappaṭum ivvakai uṇarcciyilum pārkka itu mēlāṉa taṉṟu. āṇ peṇ cērkkaiyiṉāl karu uṇṭākiṟateṉa makkaḷ aṟiyātirunta kālam oṉṟu uṇṭu. cila pūkkaḷai maṇattalāl, allatu cila vilaṅkukaḷaik kāṇutalāl, cūriyaṉiṉ katirpaṭuvatāl, āvi nuḻaitalāl karu uṇṭākiṉṟateṉa nampum piṟpōkkuṭaiya makkaḷ iṉṟum kāṇappaṭukiṉṟaṉar. āstirēliya arintā vakuppiṉar karuppat tukkuk kāraṇam āṇ peṇ cērkkaiyaṉṟu eṉa niṉaikkiṉṟaṉar. māṅkaṉiyaip pucittup peṇkaḷ karu uṇṭāṉārkaḷ eṉak kūṟum intiyap paḻaṅkataikaḷ uṇṭu. āṇ peṇ cērkkaiyiṉāl karu uṇ ṭākiṉṟateṉṟu makkaḷ aṟikiṉṟa varaiyil tantaiyaip paṟṟiya niṉaivu tōṉṟamāṭṭātu.

tāy kuḻantaimēl koḷḷumaṉpu toṭakkattil avaḷ kuḻantai aḷavil niṟkavillai; ellāk kuḻantaikaḷ aḷavilum iruntatu. ākavē muṟkālattāy taṉatu piḷḷaiyaip piṟar piḷḷaikaḷukkāka māṟṟik koḷḷavō piṟar kuḻantaikaḷait taṉ kuḻantaikaḷ pōl vaḷarkkavō viruppamuṭaiyavaḷāyiruntāḷ. eṉatu uṉatu eṉṉum eṇṇaṅkaḷ valuvaṭainta kālattil tāy aṉpu avaḷ kuḻantai aḷavil curuṅki niṟpatāyiṟṟu.

muṟkālattil āṇ peṇ toṭarpāṉa nāṇam tōṉṟi irukka muṭiyātu. uṭampu toṭarpāṉa nāṇamum āti mutal varuvataṉṟu. nirvāṇamē maṉitaṉiṉ ātinilai. āṭaiyum uṭaikaḷum kaṇṭu piṭikkappaṭṭa piṉpē nāṇamuṇṭāyiṟṟu. peṇtaṉmaikkuriya nāṇam iyaṟkaiyāka ātimutal irukkiṉṟatu. innāṇam āṇiṉ viruppai eḻuppukiṉṟatu. ēṟṟa āṇaitterintu koḷvataṟku itu utaviyāka vamaintuḷḷatu. mēlāṉa vilaṅkukaḷiṭaiyum nām ippaṇpaik kāṇalām.

āṇ peṇ campantamāṉa poṟāmai vilaṅkukaḷiṭamum kāṇap paṭukiṉṟatu. itu āti mutal uḷḷa iyaṟkai uṇarcci eṉak kūṟutalamai yātu. itu paḻaiya makkaḷ palariṭaiyē tōṉṟavillai. āṇukkum peṇṇukkum muriya vēlaikaḷ pirikkappaṭumpōtu poruḷātāram campantamāka āṇukkup peṇ tēvaippaṭukiṉṟāḷ. peṇ āṇiṭam taṅkiyirukkum nilai maṇam toṭarpāṉatu. muṟkālattil maṇameṉpatu peṇṇai āṭavaṉ taṉakku aṭimaip paṭuttutal; avvāṟu ceytal poruḷātāra oḻuṅku. poruḷātāram āṇ peṇ cērkkai uṇarcci yilum valiyatu.
kuṭumpa kālak kātal

kuṭumpa kālattilē kātal uṇarcci maṉitaṉiṉ aṟivāṟ paṇ paṭuttappaṭṭu uyarnilai aṭaikiṉṟatu. ippaṇpāṭu caṭutiyil tōṉṟa villai; ciṟitu ciṟitāka vaḷarcciyaṭaintatu. ippaṇpaṭṭa nilai āṇ peṇ poṟāmaiyiṉālum taṉippaṭṭa kātal campantattil uṇ ṭāyiṟṟu. āṇ peṇ campantamāṉa poṟāmai peṇṇaic contamāka vait tirukkumpaṭi oruvaṉait tūṇṭiṟṟu. ituvē muṟkālattil maṇattukkut tūṇṭutalāka viruntatu. ittūṇṭutalāl peṇṇai vilai koṭuttu vāṅkum vilaimaṇam eḻuntatu. ivvāṟu maṇam uṇṭāvatāl peṇ poruḷ pōl karutappaṭātu āḷpōl karutappaṭṭāḷ.

piḷḷaikaḷālum āṇ peṇ poṟāmai valivaṭaintatu. āṇ peṇ cērkkaiyiṉāl kuḻantai piṟakkiṉṟateṉa aṟiyappaṭṭapōtum, eṉatu uṉatu eṉṉum vēṟupāṭu uṇṭāṉa pōtum āṇ peṇ toṭar pāṉa poṟāmai valivaṭaintatu. kaṟpu vilaiyuṭaiyatākak koḷḷap paṭṭataṟkum kāraṇam ituvē. kaṟpu āṇ peṇ poṟāmaiyāl uṇ ṭāṉatē. itaṉāl putu muṟaiyāṉa kātal uṇarcci tōṉṟiṟṟu. paḻaiya makkaḷiṭaiyē aḻakaip paṟṟiya eṇṇam eḻavillai. makkaḷ tirutta maṭainta kālattilēyē peṇṇiṉ aḻaku kavaṉikkappaṭṭatu. peṇṇiṉ aḻaku matikkappaṭṭapōtu oḻukkamum kavaṉikkappaṭa lāyiṟṟu. muṟkālattil āṭavaṉ peṇṇaip peṇ uṟuppuṭaiya poruḷāka nōkkiṉāṉ. ippoḻutu avaṉ avaḷaip peṇ taṉmaikaḷ uṭaiya āḷākap pārkkiṉṟāṉ. pala moḻikaḷ peṇṇaik kuṟikkum coṟkaḷ avaḷiṉ irakaciya uṟuppiṉ peyarāka virukkiṉṟaṉa. nekkivō (epirēyam), jīṉā (intu jermaṉi), jeṉi (kirēkku), mōṭer (paḻaiya jermaṉi), mutaliya coṟkaḷ avvuṟuppai uṭaiyavaḷ eṉṉum poruḷuṭaiyaṉa. palkēriyattil peṇṇaik kuṟikkum ‘maṟkā’ vayiṟṟaik kuṟikkiṉṟatu. maṟṟēṉā eṉṉum peṇṇaik kuṟikkum caipīriyap peyarukku vayiṟu uṭaiyavaḷ eṉpatu poruḷ.

muṉpu peṇṇuṟuppuṭaiyavaḷāka maṉattiṟ kāṭciyaḷitta avaḷ, ippoḻutu aḻaku oḻukkam mutaliyavaiyuṭaiyavaḷākak kāṭciyaḷip pāḷāyiṉaḷ. ituvaraiyum potu nōkkāyirunta kātal ippoḻutu taṉippaṭṭa kātalāka māṟiṟṟu. vilaṅkut taṉmaiyuḷḷa kātal veḷiyē cellutal kūṭum. taṉippaṭṭa kātal veḷiyē cellamāṭṭātu.

muttam
muṟkālattil muttamiṭum vaḻakkam tōṉṟiyirukka villai. kalaṉā amerikkariṭaiyē muttattaik kuṟikkum col kāṇappaṭavillai. kuṭumpa kālattil epirēyar, kirēkkar, urōmar, arāpiyar mutaliya palariṭaiyē iv vaḻakkam tōṉṟiyiruntatu. āṉāl atu taṉ ilakkai aṭaivataṉ muṉ pala vaḻikaḷil alaintu koṇṭiruntatu. arāpiyap peṇkaḷ tamatu kaṇavaruṭaiyavum, tantaiyaruṭaiyavum tāṭikaḷai muttamiṭavēṇṭum. tammaṉaiviyar “uvaiṉ” parukiyirukkiṉṟārkaḷō eṉṟu aṟiyum poruṭṭu urōma kaṇavar tam maṉaiviyarai muttamiṭ ṭaṉar. urōmiya peṇkaḷ uvaiṉ kuṭittal kūṭātu. avarkaḷiṭaiyē vaḻaṅkiya muttaṅkaḷ palavakaiyiṉa. avai vevvēṟu peyarkaḷāl aṟiyappaṭṭaṉa. mariyātaiyiṉ poruṭṭuk koṭukkum muttam pāciyam eṉappaṭṭatu. naṭpiṉ kuṟiyākak koṭukkappaṭum muttam oskalam eṉavum, kātalukkākak koṭukkappaṭuvatu cōviyam eṉavum paṭṭaṉa. mattiya kālaṅkaḷil muttaṅkaḷ palavāṟu kaiyāḷappaṭṭaṉa. 710il pōppāṇṭavariṉ ceruppai muttamiṭum vaḻakkam tōṉṟiṟṟu. 16ām nūṟṟāṇṭil peṇkaḷ tammuṭaṉ naṭaṉamāṭiyavarkaḷukkup patil upakāramāka muttaṅkaḷ koṭuttārkaḷ. maṇamakaṉiṉ kaiyaiyum maṇamakaḷiṉ kaiyaiyum iṇaittu maṇañ ceytu vaikkum kōyiṟkuru maṇamakaḷai māttiramaṉṟu, maṇamakaḷiṉ tōḻimāraiyum muttamiṭum vaḻakkam nīṇṭakālam iruntu vantatu. naṇparum uṟaviṉarum tamatu muttaṅkaḷai māṟṟik koṇṭārkaḷ; aṉṉiyarāyiṉ kaiyil muttamiṭap paṭṭatu. iṟutiyil muttam taṉakkuriya iṭattai aṭaintatu. tāyiṉ muttamum, kātal muttamumē iṉṟu nilaittirukkiṉṟaṉa. putu muṟaiyāṉa kātalukku muttam mukkiyam. atu iṉṟik kātalai aṟiya muṭiyātu. uṟaviṉarai muttamiṭum vaḻakkam nāḷvītam maṟaintu varukiṉṟatu.

cōviyat uruciyāvil tirumaṇac caṭṭam
cōviyat uruciyāvil putiya tirumaṇaccaṭṭam 1927ām āṇṭu jaṉavari mātam mutalām nāḷ naṭaimuṟaikku vantatu. cār kālattil maṉaivi kaṇavaṉiṉ cottāka viruntāḷ. avaḷ peyar kaṇavaṉiṉ “pās pōṭṭil” pativu ceyyappaṭṭiruntatu. avaḷ kaṇavaṉai viṭṭup piriya vēṇṭumāyiṉ avaḷukkuḷḷa “pās pōṭ” vēṇṭum. ataṉaik koṭuppataṟkuk kaṇavaṉ perumpālum maṟuttāṉ. avaḷ taṉiyē taṉatu pās pōṭṭaip peṟṟuk koḷḷa vēṇṭumāyiṉ kaṇa vaṉiṉ cammatam vēṇṭum eṉṉum caṭṭam iruntatu. nāṭṭukku veḷiyē tirumaṇam ceyvatu muṟaikēṭāṉa maṇamākak karutappaṭṭatu.

kōyilkaḷil niṟaivēṟṟappaṭum maṇaṅkaḷ māttiram caṭṭa cammatamākaviruntaṉa. āṇ 18 vayatukkum peṇ 16 vayatukkum muṉṉum tirumaṇam ceytal kūṭātu. tirumaṇa oppantam peṟṟōriṉ cammatattaip poṟuttiruntatu. avarkaḷiṉ cammatamiṉṟi maṇañ ceytal kuṟṟamākak karutappaṭṭatu. kuṟṟañ ceytavarukku eṭṭumātac ciṟait taṇṭaṉai vitikkappaṭṭatu. yūtar, piṟa matattavar, camayattil nampikkaiyillātavar kaḷōṭu ceyyum maṇam taṭukkappaṭṭiruntatu. mattiyakāla uruciyac caṭṭam kūṟuvatu piṉvarumāṟu: “kaṇavaṉum maṉaiviyum orumittu vāḻutal vēṇṭum. kaṇavaṉ oru iṭattiliruntu iṉṉōriṭattukku vāḻumporuṭṭu ēkiṉāl maṉaivi avaṉaip piṉtoṭarntu cella vēṇṭum. maṉaivi kaṇavaṉaik kuṭumpat talaivaṉākak koṇṭu avaṉukku aṭaṅki naṭattal vēṇṭum. maṉaiviyiṉ kaṭamai kaṇavaṉiṉ eṇṇattukku aṭaṅki naṭattal.”

putiya tirumaṇac caṭṭam tāyaiyum piḷḷaiyaiyum kāppataṟkum, kaṇavaṉ maṉaivi iruvarukkum cottu, piḷḷaikaḷiṉ kalvi mutaliya vaikaḷil urimai koṭuppataṟkum eḻuntatu. āṇukkum peṇṇukkum campantappaṭṭa ellā nikaḻccikaḷum taṉippaṭṭa karumaṅkaḷākak koḷḷap paṭṭaṉa. cōviyat uruciyāvil pativu ceyyappaṭṭa maṇaṅkaḷaip pōlavē pativu ceyyappaṭāta maṇaṅkaḷum caṭṭa cammatam peṟṟuḷḷaṉa. tirumaṇa nīkkam campantamāṉa nikaḻccikaḷil aracāṭci talaiyiṭum pōtu tirumaṇap pativu, tirumaṇattai attāṭcippaṭutta utaviyaḷikkiṉṟatu. kaliyāṇap pativu illāta maṇaṅkaḷil piṇakku uṇṭākum pōtu kaliyāṇattaip paṟṟic cāṉṟupaṭuttum kaṭamai uṇṭākiṉṟatu. kaliyāṇam taṉippaṭṭa karumamātaliṉ oruvaṉ pala peṇkaḷai maṇap patil taṭai irukkavillai. oru maṇam ceytavaṉ allatu ceytavaḷ ak kaliyāṇa nīkkattukku muṉ maṟumaṇam ceytal muṭiyātu. orumaṇam ceytavar ammaṇattait taḷḷātu poy vākkuk koṭuttu maṟumaṇam ceytāl ataṟkākat taṇṭaṉai aṭaintār.

uruciyāvil viyapicārattukkut taṇṭaṉaiyillai. atu kaṇavaṉ maṉaiviyariṉ taṉippaṭṭa karumam. nāṭṭukkut tīṅku viḷaikkakkūṭiya āṇ peṇ cērkkait toṭarpāṉa ceykaikaḷil araciṉar talaiyiṭu kiṉṟaṉar. vaippāṭṭi vaittal, muṟaikēṭāṉa āṇ peṇ cērkkai mutaliyavaikaḷil araciṉar talaiyiṭuvatillai. kaliyāṇap pativil kiriyaikaḷ yātum illai. iru pakutiyiṉarum virumpiṉāl tamatu naṇpar kaḷaikkūṭa irukkumpaṭi avviṭattukku aḻaikkalām. kaliyāṇap pativu ceyvataṟku irupālarum patiṉeṭṭu vayatukkup patiyātavarkaḷāka viruttal vēṇṭum. iruvarum tām kaliyāṇam ceytu koḷḷa virumpuvatākat tamatu viruppattait terivikka vēṇṭum. pativut tāḷil kaiyeḻuttiṭukkiṉṟa iruvarum appeyaruṭaiyavarkaḷ eṉṟu at tāṭcippaṭutta vēṇṭum. iruvarukkum maṟṟavariṉ uṭal nilaiyaippaṟṟi aṟivikkappaṭṭuḷḷa teṉṟu uṟuti kūṟavēṇṭum. iruvaril oruvar maṉam palavīṉam allatu maṉakkōḷāṟu uḷḷavarāyiṉ kaliyāṇam pativu ceyyappaṭamāṭṭātu. peṇ allatu āṇ, pativu nilaiyattukkuc cella muṭiyātu nōyuṟṟiruntāl vīṭṭil maṇap pativu ceytu koḷḷa lām. maṇattukkup piṉ putiya peyar iṭṭuk koḷḷa virumpiṉālum avarkaḷ ceytu koḷḷalām. kaṇavaṉ maṉaiviyiṉ peyarai virumpi ṉāl vaittukkoḷḷalām. poyyāṉa vāyt takaval koṭuttiruntāl avarkaḷukku vitikkappaṭum taṇṭaṉaiyaik kuṟitta caṭṭam avar kaḷukku vācittuk kāṭṭappaṭum. kaliyāṇam ceytu koḷḷum āṇ peṇkaḷōṭu pativukkārarum kaiyeḻuttiṭuvar. kaṭcikaḷ virumpiṉāl cāṭcikaḷiṉ muṉpu kaiyeḻuttiṭappaṭum. paṭiyāta iruvarukkiṭaiyil pativu naṭappataṉāl iraṇṭu cāṭcikaḷ vēṇṭum.

iruvarum tām tām virumpiya vēlaikaḷaic ceyyalām. iruvarum cērntu vīṭṭai naṭattutal potuvāṉa oḻuṅku. oruvar virumpiṉāl tamatu iruppiṭattai māṟṟik koḷḷalām. tirumaṇattukku muṉ iruvarukkum avarkaḷiṭamirunta cottup potuvāṉataṉṟu. tirumaṇattukkup piṉ iruvarum tēṭiya poruḷ iruvarukkum potu vāṉatu. pākam araciṉarāl tīrmāṉikkappaṭum. ivvurimai kaliyāṇam pativu ceyyappaṭṭavarkaḷukkum, kaliyāṇam pativu ceyyappaṭātavar kaḷukkum uṇṭu. tirumaṇam nilaittirukkum varaiyil oruvar maṟṟavaraik kāppāṟṟak kaṭamaippaṭṭavar. cōviyat uruciyāvil tirumaṇaṅkaḷ ilakuvil kulaittuviṭattakkaṉa. kaliyāṇap pativiṟ pōlavē iruvarum kaiyeḻuttiṭumpōtu kaliyāṇam kulaintu viṭukiṉṟatu. kaliyāṇattai nīkkuvatu aracāṅkamaṉṟu; iruvariṉ viruppamumē. aracāṅkam iruvariṉ viruppattaiyum pativu ceytu vaikkiṉṟatu. cōviyat uruciyāvil kaliyāṇa nīkka vaḻakkukaḷ illai; kaliyāṇa nīkkattukkuk kāraṇamum vēṇṭiyatillai. iruvar virumpiṉālum, oruvar virumpiṉālum kaliyāṇa nīkkam ceyyappaṭalām. kaliyāṇattaip pōlak kaliyāṇa nīkkamum taṉippaṭṭa karumam. kaliyāṇa nīkkam ceytavarkaḷ virumpiṉāl maṟupaṭi kaliyāṇam ceytu koḷḷalām. oruvar ittaṉai muṟaitāṉ kaliyāṇam ceyyalām allatu kaliyāṇa nīkkam ceyyalām eṉṟu caṭṭam illai. iruvaril oruvar uḻaippillātiruntāl uḻaikkiṉ ṟavar avaraik kāppāṟṟak kaṭamaippaṭṭavar; kaliyāṇa nīkkattukkuppiṉ vēlai ceyyat takutiyaṟṟavarai oru āṇṭum, takutiyuḷḷavarai āṟu mātamum kāppāṟṟa vēṇṭum. kaliyāṇa nīkkattukku muṉ yār kuḻantai kaḷaik kāppaṟṟuvatu eṉṟu tīrmāṉikkappaṭukiṉṟatu. itil muṭivu kāṇā viṭṭāl aracāṅkam tīrppaḷikkiṉṟatu. kuḻantaikaḷ ēḻu allatu eṭṭu vayatu aṭaiyum varaiyum atu tāyuṭaṉ iruppatu vaḻakkam. piḷḷaikaḷ tāyōṭum tantaiyōṭum viṭappaṭāviṭṭāl kuḻantaikaḷiṉ viṭutiyil viṭappaṭuvārkaḷ. iruvarum tamatu uḻaippukkut takkavāṟu avarkaḷukku utaviyaḷikkiṟārkaḷ. 1902 varaiyil kaliyāṇamākāta tāymāriṉ kuḻantaikaḷ tāykkō tantaikkō contamillātiruntaṉa: iṅkilāntu, jermaṉi, pirāṉcu mutaliya nāṭukaḷil avarkaḷ tāykkuc contamāyirun tārkaḷ. uruciyāvil muṟaiyāṉa piḷḷai muṟaiyillāta piḷḷai eṉṟu vēṟupāṭu illai. tantai oruvaṉ taṉ mūlam kuḻantai uṇṭākavillai eṉak kūṟi maṟuttāl karuppamāyirukkum tāy piḷḷaip pēṟṟukku mūṉṟu mātaṅkaḷiṉ muṉ tirumaṇam patiyum atikārikku ataṉai aṟivittal vēṇṭum. avaḷ tāṉ karuppamaṭainta kālattaiyum tantaiyiṉ irup piṭattaiyum terivittal vēṇṭum. tirumaṇappativu atikāri aṟivippup pōkkiya oru tiṅkaḷuḷ, peṇ kūṟiyatu poyyāyiruntāl avaṉ kōrṭṭu mūlam maṟukkalām. avvāṟu ceyyāviṭṭāl avaṉ atai ēṟṟukkoṇṭatākap poruḷ ceyyappaṭum. avaḷiṉ aṟivippup poyyākaviruntāl atai maṟuttu oru āṇṭukkuḷ vaḻakkut toṭara lām. avarkaḷ ‘kōrṭṭil’ uṇmai pēca vēṇṭum. illāviṭil poyc cattiyak kuṟṟam cāṭṭappaṭuvar. peṇ pala āṭavarōṭu cērkkai uṭaiyavaḷeṉṟum kuḻantaiyiṉ tantai yār eṉa aṟiya muṭiyātu eṉṟum avaṉ kūṟiṉāl ellāt tantaiyarumē avaḷ piḷḷaip pēṟṟuc celavaiyum piḷḷai vaḷarppuc celavaiyum koṭukka vēṇṭum. itaṉāl kuḻantai pala tantaiyaruṭaiyateṉṉum avamāṉattuṭpaṭu meṉak karutip palaruḷ atika poruḷ koṭukkak kūṭiyavaṉē celavaik koṭukkumpaṭi kaṭṭaḷaiyiṭappaṭuvāṉ. ukkiraiṉ kuṭumpaṅkaḷil ivvakai nikaḻccikku ellāt tantaimārum cērntu celavu kaṭṭutal vēṇṭum. peṇṇai oruvaṉ aṭimaippaṭuttuvatil toṭaṅkiya tirumaṇam iṉṟu innilai varaiyil vaḷarcciyaṭaintuḷḷatu.

anākarika nākarika muṉaikaḷiṉ cantippu
ulakilē uruciya nāṭṭilēyē peṇkaḷ āṇkaḷ ottavar kaḷākak kaṭṭuppāṭiṉṟi vāḻkiṉṟārkaḷ. iṅku peṇkaḷiṟ palar maṇañ ceytu koḷḷa virumpuvatillai; āṉāl piḷḷaikaḷaip peṟa virumpu kiṟārkaḷ. ivvāṟu kaliyāṇamillāmalē piḷḷaikaḷaip peṟṟu vāḻum peṇkaḷiṉ eṇ atikappaṭṭu varukiṉṟatu. māskō nakarattil uyarnta pataviyiluḷḷa peṇ orutti kūṟiyiruppatu varumāṟu: “eṉakku mūṉṟu piḷḷaikaḷ irukkiṉṟaṉar. nāṉ iṉṉum oṉpatu piḷḷaikaḷai vevvēṟu āṭavar mūlam peṟa virumpukiṉṟēṉ.”
uruciyāvilē kaliyāṇamākip piṟanta piḷḷai, kaliyāṇamā kāmal piṟanta piḷḷai eṉṉum vēṟupāṭillai. peṇkaḷ virumpiṉāl karuvai aḻittuk koḷḷalām. itaṟku ellāp paṭṭiṉaṅkaḷilum maruttuva viṭutikaḷ araciṉarāl amaikkappaṭṭuḷḷatu.

namatu nāṭṭu nilai
intiya nāṭṭil oruvaṉ pala maṉaiviyarai maṇantu koḷḷalām eṉṉum viti mikap paḻaiya nāḷ mutal iṉṟu varaiyum iruntu varukiṉṟatu. oru peṇ oruvaṉukku maṉaiviyāki viṭṭāl avaḷukkuk kaṇavaṉiṭam viruppam iruntāleṉṉa, illāviṭṭāleṉṉa avaḷ kaṭaici varaiyil avaṉatu maṉaiviyākavē iruntuvara vēṇṭum. avaḷ kaliyāṇa nīkkam ceytu koḷḷa aṉumatikkappaṭamāṭṭāḷ. kaṇavaṉ iṟantu viṭṭāl avaḷ maṟumaṇam ceytu koḷḷātu iṟantu pōṉavaṉiṉ maṉaiviyākavē iruntu avaṉiṭattil patti celuttik koṇṭu irukka vēṇṭum. maṉaivi iṟantuviṭṭāl kaṇavaṉ virumpiya paṭi iṉṉoru maṉaiviyaiyō pala maṉaiviyaraiyō maṇantu koḷḷalām. mika nākarikam aṭaintuḷḷavarkaḷ eṉak kūṟikkoḷḷum intiya makkaḷukku itu niyāyamāṉa ceyalākavē tōṉṟi varuvatāyiṟṟu. kaṇavaṉ iṟantuviṭṭāl peṇkaḷai uṭaṉkaṭṭai ēṟṟuvatākiya tīya vaḻakkamum namatu nāṭṭil iruntu vantatu. uṭaṉ kaṭṭai ēṟṟuvateṉpatu iṟantu pōṉa kaṇavaṉōṭu maṉaiviyai uyirōṭu vaittuk koḷutti viṭuvatu. uṭaṉkaṭṭai ēṟa virumpāta peṇkaḷ palavantamāka iḻuttuc ceṉṟu uṭaṉ kaṭṭai ēṟṟappaṭṭārkaḷ eṉṟu viḷakki eḻutiyuḷḷārkaḷ. ikkoṭiya vaḻakkam āṅkilarāl oḻikkappaṭṭatu.

āṅkilar āṭci uṇṭāṉa piṉpē peṇkaḷukkuk kalvi kaṟkum vāyppuk kiṭaittatu. iṉṟu intiya jaṉat tokaiyil nūṟṟukku pattu vītattiṉarē eḻutap paṭikka aṟivārkaḷ. ivarkaḷuḷ mikac cilarē kalvi kaṟṟa peṇkaḷāvār. mēlnāṭṭu muṟaiyāṉa kalviyiṉāl peṇkaḷ viḻippaṭaintaṉar. mēlnāṭṭu muṟaiyil tamakkum āṇkaḷai otta cutantaram vēṇṭumeṉap peṇkaḷ vātāṭukiṉṟaṉar. iṉṟu tiruṇam campantamāṉa tiruttaṅkaḷ pala uṇṭāyirukkiṉṟaṉa. peṇkaḷ virumpi ṉāl kaliyāṇa nīkkam ceytukoḷḷum caṭṭam uṇṭāyirukkiṉṟatu. peṇkaḷ āṇkaḷaippōlavē āciriyarākavum, aracāṅka aluvalāḷa rākavum, piṟavāṟum poruḷīṭṭattakkavarākip poruḷātāra viṭutalai aṭaintu varukiṉṟamaiyāl intiya nāṭṭilum kātal maṇaṅkaḷ nikaḻkiṉṟaṉa. peṇkaḷaik kīḻmaippaṭuttiyum kaṭṭuppaṭuttiyum vaikkum koḷkaikaḷ nāḷ vītam maṟaintu varukiṉṟaṉa. mēṟkut tēcaṅ kaḷil ciṟuvarukkuk kaṭṭāya ārampak kalviyuṇṭu? namatu nāṭṭil itaip paṟṟiya eṇṇam iṉṉum utikkavillai. namatu nāṭṭil kaṭṭāyak kalvi uṇṭāki makkaḷ aṟivu vaḷarcciyaṭaiyumpōtē camūkattukku naṉmai payavāta paḻaṅ koḷkaikaḷ maṟaintu oḻiyum; iyaṟkaikkēṟṟa tirumaṇaṅ kaḷum naṭaipeṟum. iyaṟkai maṇameṉpatu peṇ kaṇavaṉait terivatu; āṇ tāṉ virumpiya peṇṇait terivataṉṟu; peṇ tāṉ virumpiya āṇait terivatē vilaṅkukaḷ paṟavaikaḷ mutaliya uyir vāḻvaṉa ellāvaṟṟuḷḷum kāṇappaṭuvatu.

* Among mammal and other animals a periodic rut is general throught universal. It is a fact that spring and the harvest are among the savages, barabarians and the
modern peasants, regular seasons both for general festivity and for special development of sexual feelings. The reasonmay be partly biological, partly climatic and mostly connected with mass food supply - Studies of Savages - p.91. - Earnest Crowley.
“kuṭṭikkut tāyp pālkoṭuttu vaḷarkkum vilaṅkukaḷukkum piṟa uyirkaḷukkum āṇṭiṉ cila kālaṅkaḷil āṇ peṇ cērkkai toṭarpāṉa matam uṇṭākiṉṟatu. itu ulakam muḻumaiyilum kāṇappaṭukiṉṟatu. ilai tuḷirkālattum tāṉiya aṟuvaṭai kālattum, kāṭṭuc cātiyiṉarum ikkāla nāṭṭu makkaḷum tamatu viḻākkaḷaik koṇṭāṭukiṉṟaṉar. ak kālaṅkaḷil āṇ peṇ cērkkai uṇarcciyum vaḷarukiṉṟatu. ik kāraṇattiṉ pakuti pirāṇikaḷiṉ iyaṟkai campantamum, pakuti uṇavu, veppa nilaikaḷumāka virukkalām.”
1. Sexual selection among animals is supposed, by Darwin to be exercisd by females and not by males - The Mothers.
2. Among animals the female admits the male at such times only as she is prepared for the exercise of his function. At other times she repels him, and her attitude towards him is generally one of positive hostility. The description given by pallas of the mannar in which the female camel rounds on the bull the moment she is impregnated, driving him away with much snarling and display of teeth. Except on the moment when she herself is on a state of rut serves Dr. Tiller the female absolutely refuses any sexual intercourse’ -Ibid.
3. After the function is fulfilled there appears to be as a general rule an actual repulsion between the sexes, “As soon as the pairing is over” says Brehm
speaking of mammals Generally, great indifference is shown towards one another by the sexes. - The Mother Vol. 1. p.122.
4. What sexual selection exists among the lower aces is predominantly exercised by the women - Ibid.
5. It is claimed by some writers that in certain parts of the world there have existed tribal communties comprised exclusively of women who work, fig and hunt like men. The males live apart in seperate community . Periodically, once or twice a year at a fixed time, the men are perimitted to visit or meet their amazon wives for a certian number of days, after which they seperate once more and go back to live in thier own communities. The amazons lived under the rule of a queen and were governed by their own laws and customs. Children which resulted from this meeting, if female, were brought up by their mothers and be came part of the amazon nation ; if male, they were either slain or sent to the Garareans - Amatory Curiosa - p.3.
6. Kiss has developed out of love bite. In many parts of Europe women are not convinced of their lovers or husband’s affection unless their own lovers leave the visible marks of it in the form of incesions from their teeth - The Mothers Vol. 2. p.16.
muttam, oruvakaip pēccu eṉavum koḷḷappaṭukiṉṟatu. itaṉai in nūliṉ piṟitōriṭattiṟ kāṇka.
The gradual admixure of tenderness with the mating instinct, its transformation into love is a pace which has taken place in the psychological evolution of the female, and it appears probable that in the human species love was at first confined to mothers - The Mothers Vol - p. 123. 
Sensual love is fickle; it is influenced by the desire of change. On the otherhand, when the love implies sympathy and affection arising from mental qualities there is a he between husband and wife which lasts long after youth and beauty are gone. This leads to monogamy that is enduring - A short History of Marriage. p. 247.
7. The women food gatherers became the first plant cultivators and the men, apart from lending some assistance in preparing the garden continued as hunters. This generally admitted as affording the best explanation of why among primitive plant cultivators the system of descent is generally of the maternal type i.e. Kinship is reckoned in the female line - Ecology - T.W.Bews - p.179.
8. The fatherhood is not taken in to account when the sexual bond is a loose one, and undersuch circumstances the descent can only be reckoned on the mother side. The sense of father-hood was a later development and in primative times the child was held to be allied by blood with the mother and not with the father - Primitive Family - C.N. Starke.
The wife though she should grieve at her husband’s death though aften she really and sincerely does so remains but a stranger by the rules of maternal kinship. A woman and her brother are bound to each other by characteristic and highly important ties of kinship. The brother became the natural warder of her children, who therefore have to reqard him and not their father as the legal head of the family-Crime and Custom in Savage Life - pp.34,34 - B.Malinowski.
9. In Ladakh women who have two, three, four or even five husbands, have generally use the right of selecting additional husbands or lovers who are known
as “Magfa”. The supplementing husband is regarded as the property of the wife; she can dismiss him whenever she chooses and take another. The women as a general rule avail themselves of these privileges; among the Thodas also the women exercise the right to have recognised lovers in addition to their regular husbands. In Ceylon according to Tennant the women of nobler birth and wealth had the most husbands. So elsewhere in the Marquses it was with princesses that polyandry on the most extensive scale was practised. Amongst the Aleuts according to Bancript, it was the wealthiest women and those whose position was therefore of greatest infuluence who were the most polyandrous in their relations. Noble woman and heiresses of Madagascar have many lovers as they choose. In Arabia high born women refused to bend themselves of one man by marriage and claimed complete freedom in the disposal of their favours - The Mothers Vol. 2 - p. 258.
“lāṭāk nāṭṭup peṇkaḷ iraṇṭu, mūṉṟu allatu nāṉku aintu kaṇavarait terinteṭuttuk koḷkiṉṟaṉar. avarkaḷukku meṉmēlum kaṇavarait terin teṭukkum urimai uṇṭu. avarkaḷ terinteṭukkum kaṇavarukku mekkavā eṉpatu peyar. mēlum terinteṭukkum kaṇavar peṇṇiṉ cottākak karutappaṭukiṉṟaṉar. avaḷ virumpiya entanēramum kaṇavaṉait turattiviṭalām. tōṭar vakuppup peṇ kaḷukkum ivvakai atikāram uṇṭu. ilaṅkaiyil uyarkulap peṇkaḷum cīmāṭṭi kaḷumē atika kaṇavarai uṭaiyavarkaḷāyiruntaṉar eṉṟu teṉaṟ kūṟiyuḷḷār. mār kucas eṉṉum tēcattil araca kuṭumpap peṇkaḷē pala kaṇavarai vaittiruntārkaḷ. alueṟ eṉṉum nāṭṭil cīmāṭṭikaḷē atika kaṇavarai uṭaiyarāyiruntaṉar. maṭakāskaril cīmāṭṭikaḷ tām viruppiya pala kaṇavarait terinteṭuppārkaḷ. arāpiyāvil uyarkulap peṇkaḷ oruvaṉukkup paṇivatai maṟuttārkaḷ; avarkaḷ tāṅkaḷ virumpiyavāṟu tamatu tayavai virumpiya piṟarukku aḷikkum cutantiram peṟṟiruntārkaḷ.”
10. The adultary is an offence under patriarchal but not under matriarchal conditions. - Ibid p. 207.
11. Polyandry, the traces of which are still to be found among many castes and tribes of southern India was not prohibited among the Nayars until, as late as nineteenth century. The maximum number of legitimate husbands for a noble Nayar lady according to various reports seems to have been twelve. Each husband had the right to remain with his wife in turn, punctually regulated and reckoned in turn punctually from mid-day ot mid-day. - Mother Right in India. p.61 Baran Omar Ralf Ehreneels.
Under the Kandian laws polyandry as well as polgamy was allowed without limitation as to the number of husbands or wives. A women usually had two husbands and there are cases where women had as many as seven. The custom of polyandry as practised in Ceylon ....J.A.Gunaratna.
12. The older and important deity is always the female partner of the divine couple. Everywhere she is unwed...Mother Right in India - p,190.
13. The actual leader which guides the herd in its movements and keeps watch against possible dangers is usually an old female. The baffalo herds are likwise led by an old cow. The female leadership is common to most if not all horned ruminants. There is in fact one way, and one only by the feminine constitution of the family could have been maintained while it expanded at the same time into a larger assembly, namely, by the sons leaving the group and daughters remaining and pairing with males from some other group - The Mothers Vol.2.p.185.
14. The Mothers Vol. p. 454.
15. Where (in Egypt) women held a position more free and more honourable than they in any country today, Diodorus affirms that in a Egyptian family it is the man who is subjected to women. “All this explain why the Queen received more power and respect than the king, and why among private individuals als, the woman rules over the man, and that it is stipulated between married couples by the terrors of the marrying contract that the man shall obey the woman” (Diodorus)- The position of Woman in premitive Society.
16. Ibid.
17. Universal History of the World Vol. II.
The ancient Egyptian woman had their own right of property; the sons were named after their mothers; the ancient inscriptions more often show a genealogy of the mothers than that of the father’s property. Right of princes were inherited by the daughter not by the son’s son. As late as the 4th century there existed in Egypt a matriarchal side, with matriarcahal form of marriage enabling a girl to choose her husband herself and to divorse him after a payment of fixed fine - Mother Right in India. P. 194.
18. Matriarchal culture circle is now considered to have extended over a larger period commencing earlier and longer in the course of Evolution - Mother Right in India. P. 8.
19. My study in the palaeo-ethnology and prehistoric anthropology have shown me that women has played greater part in the progess of civilization in the softening of manners - prof. D.E. Mortiller-Year book of facts.
Woman becomes in general tenderhearted, merciful, compassionate towards all males- towards animals and all living things; and even towards plants, flowers, in animale things, possessions which are handed gently and tenderly, whereas the male is disposed to be rough, to destory and break - The Mothers Vol. 2 - p. 146.
Not a few reasons remain for seeking a fresh term say Matri-primal? to express the highly important and in various ways even predominant influence of women in early society. As from the well founded traditions of mother goddess to the substantial initiatives and attributions of women as inventor and civilizer of these conspicuously in the domestic and in the agricultural life; as well as educator of children and counseller of the tribe, as markedly among Red Indian tribes, so far even to this day among other survivals the matrilinear family system of the Nairs of S.W. India is conspicuously associated with a high status of woman, and a good deal of civilization while its antiquity and that in high esteem is further confirmed by the tradition of various ancient civilizations and religions - Life outlines of general Biology - P. 1188 - Arthur Thomson.
20. Individual marriage has its foundation in economic relations. Individual economic associations between sexual partners have inevitably tended to establish individual sexual claims. The married woman tends in time to become to be prohibited to all but to her individual associate...Marriage by giving a woman to her husband merely separates her in common with others...It has been supposed that exclusive personal claims over women arise originally in the most premitive human societies in consequence of masculine jealousy. Matriarchal society survived until the dawn of culture brought about by contact with the rich civilization of the Oment; and the man came as a suitor to the woman, through her alone he could enter in to possessions of the land...Women had long ceased to be cultivators of the soil. Their traditional ownership of it by transmission of property in the feminine line, was, as have been seen readily circum-verted and reduced to legal function. They regular and assured agricultural food supply had released the men... The corn field abolished the economic importance of the hunter. The economic function of the female came to an end. He was set free for other avocations. He had yoked his oxen to the plough; he had by degrees taken over the bulk of agricultural labour. The industries which had hitherto been exclusively in the woman’s hands passed in to those of men. The household crafts which had originated with the household worder, pottery, woodwork, rude building, weaving etc, became masculine industries. They became generally extended through the trading activities of the caravan driver and the seafarer. The fertility of the land had released the men; they took over the work of the women. The latter from being the workers were left idle. Thus the great economic revolution brought about by agriculture which is accentuated for a while the matriarchal character of the archaiasocieties which transformed had ultimately of the opposite result. In the end no economic change established male supremacy more firmly and overthrow matriarchal society so completely. The sexual divison of labour upon which the social development had been founded in premitive societies was abolished. Women instead of being the chief producer, became economically unproductive, destitute and dependent.
The contact between the toiling premitive woman and the idle lady of civilization has been mistaken for an indication of enslavement of the former and the freedom of the latter marks the opposite relation. It is the premitive toiler who is independent and the employed woman who has lost her freedom, and is destitute - The Mothers Vol. 2 - p.p.50, 51.
21. We can see the growth of purchase marriage in the service demand by the parents of the bride, this taking the place of the earliest custom of the bridegroom proving his fitness by test of strength - ñaṭiḷavaiiṭin ṭika ṟaṭiayan in yīcaiavaiiena 
society - p. 248.
22. The law of female inheritance must at one time have been universal...The husband’s property was made over by dead to the wife (atfrist entierlly and afterwards in part) to secure its inheritance by the children of the marriage. It was in such wise way the Egyptians arranged the difficult problem of fusing of mother right with father right - Position of women in primitive society -p.p.249,214.
* The later rise of patriarchal system and its spread as a social institution has been clearly associated with a different theory of the role of the sexes. For here the dominant importance is given ot the father whose all important seed is shown in the woman’s field; a conception which goes naturally with the subordination of woman to man and must have generally helped to justify it even in her own eyes-Life outlines of general Biology.
* The Agama favours both sexes equally, and makes no distinction between women and men for men and woman compose equally mankind. This sect holds women in great esteem and calls them Sakthi-that is a woman -. To illtreat the Sakthi is held to be a crime . Principles of Tantra P - XVIII - Avalon.
23. The pastoral clan or tribe is usually patriarchal and aristocratic men keeping their power in their own hands. The office of tribal chief in most cases is hereditary. Woman kind was placed in a position of subjection - Human Ecology - p. 227 - J.W.Bews.
24. Among the ancient Romans in relation to the house father, all in the household were destitute of legal right. The wife and the children no less than the bullack or the slave - A Short History of marriage p. 137 - Edward Westermark.
25. Adultery however was not a sin against morality but a trespass against the husband -The Assyrian laws -G.R.Driver and John C Miles.
26. Morals vary, therefore, as laws vary according to peoples and to times. The rules of morality form a second cord set above to social law, and they embody the larger aggrigate of duties-The World History Vol.1-p. 46.
We have examples of general promiscuity, and examples of modified promiscuity, in which, with the pretence of marriage, the woman may bestow her favours upon any one, under certain restrictions as to rank and family. We have numerous examples of polyandry and they are such as to show that the polyandry must be regarded as a modification of advance from promiscuity-Primitive Marriage - p. 94-John Ferguson Mc.Lenned.
Spartan husband had no scruple in calling on a friend, or even a stranger to be the father of his children; it was proper for a Spartan matron to be mistress in two houses - Premitive marriage p. 10. - John Ferguson. Mc. Lennan.
It was their custom to surrender them even when they had two or three children by them, to other men, as Cato surrendered Marcia in our own time - Strabo.
27. Another custom which has been adduced as evidence of former communism in women is that which requires a man to offer his wife or one of his wives to a guest.... The custom of lending wives were found among many peoples of different parts. This sort of hospitality is frequently mentioned in Irish heroic tales - A short history of marriage - p.14 - Edward Westermark.
For it is equally shared by the ancient Greeks and Romans who sold or lent their-wives to their friends either for pleasure or procreation. So plutarch describing the life of Lycurgas the Spartan law-giver wrote, “He laughed at those who revenge with wars and blood-shed the communication of a married woman’s favours; and allowed that if a man in years should have a young wife he might introduce to her some handsome and honest young man, whom he most approved of and when she had a child of the venerous race bring, bring it up as his own. On the other hand, he allowed that if a man of character should entertain a passion for a married women on account of her modesty and the beauty of her children he might treat with her husband for admission of her company, that so planting under beauty bearing soil, he might produce excellent children, the congeanial offspring of excellent parents. Sycurgas justifies his regulation on the ground that children were not so much the property of parents as of the state as the state was interested in seeing that they were begotten by the best men in it ! The regulation was held to have had the most salutary effect upon the morals of the women themselves. The privilege of lending and receiving a wife esteemed a high privilege of the Sparten citizen and its forfeiture was deemed condigh punishment reserved for serious delinquencies. The lending of wives reserved for serious delinquencies. The lending of wives to friends was regarded as one mark of favour in Athens, and Socretes is said to have lent his wife Xantippe to his young disciple and friend Alcibisdes. That the wife was friendship’s offering is illustrated in the life of Cato the younger. when he married his second wife Marria after divorcing Atlia, his friend Hortensius a man of great dignity and politeness requested Cato to lend him his married daughter Portia for the purpose of propagation. Cato answered that he had the greatest regard for the friendship of Hortensius but he could not think of his application for another man’s wife. Whereupon Hortensius requested him to lend his own wife which Cato did not only lent but present to his friend with the consent of the lady’s father - The Hindu code p. XX - Dr.H.S.GOUR.
An Eskimo may oblige a vistor by surrendering his wife temporarily, and a man coming from one settlement to another expects to be similarly treated by a member of his own age - An Introduction to cultural anthropology - p. 244 - Robert H. Lowill.
It may be well to append some modern examples of promiscuity, and of practices which have the same effect in rendering uncertain male parentage. The
Ansarians have their wives in common; the Kaias (paropamisans) lend their wives to their guests; so do the Eimauk (Caubul), so we are informed of the Kandyans. The Mpongme (Africa) lend wives Premitive marriage p. 96.
Exchange of wives are habitual among Eskimos. This is due often as a matter of convenience.... If a man’s wife is unable to accompany him on a hunting party, he will exchange her for the wife of some men who is staying behind, - The Mothers Vol, 2 - p.10.
28. In some districts of Holland girls it is said are seldom virgins at their marriage and are often in an advanced state of pregnancy at the ceremony. Prenuptual unchastity, multiplicity of lovers and illequimate children are not looked upon as a bar to marriage. Similar views are said to remain in the rural parts of Austria, Baveria, Switzerland, and Norway. In many parts of Italy says a sixteenth century writer the men would not think of marrying a woman who had not given proof of her popularity by the multitude of her armours. The mothers Vol. 3 - p. 313.
ollāntiṉ cila pakutikaḷil tirumaṇamākum kālattil kaṉṉiyarāka virukkum peṇkaḷ ariyar. peṇ karukkoṇṭiruttal, pala kaṇavaraiyuṭaiyavaḷāyiruttal pōlvaṉa avaḷ tirumaṇattukku iṭaiyūṟukaḷākamāṭṭā. āstiriyā, pavēriyā, cuviṟcarlāntu, nārvē mutaliya nāṭukaḷilum ik karuttē nilavi varukiṉṟatu. patiṉāṟām nūṟṟāṇṭil vāḻnta eḻuttāḷar oruvar ittāliyiṉ pala pakutikaḷil peṇ orutti taṉakkup pala kātalarkaḷ uḷar eṉpatai attāṭcip paṭuttiṉālaṉṟi avaḷai āṭavaṉ maṇantukoḷḷa niṉaikkamāṭṭāṉ” eṉṟu kūṟiyuḷḷār.

29. But also among races having a fully developed married institution the unmarried are often permitted to have in conditions of sexual freedom.... The institution of child betrothal marked an important step in development; for in such cases the brides was from childhood destined for her husband and bound to chastity. This is the historical significance of child betrothal - The world History Vol. 1 - p. 50.

tirumaṇa oḻuṅkukaḷ uruppaṭṭuḷḷa nāṭukaḷilum maṇamākāta peṇkaḷukku avarkaḷ virumpiya āṭavarukkut tamatu tayavai aḷikkum cutantiram iruntuvantatu. ciṟu piḷḷaikaḷāka irukkumpōtu avarkaḷukkut tirumaṇam niccayam ceyyappaṭuvatāl avarkaḷ iḷamai toṭakkam tam kaṇavarukkē urimaippaṭṭuk kaṟpāyirukkap paḻakiṉārkaḷ. ciṟu piḷḷait tirumaṇattiṉ irakaciyam ituvē.

30. Group Marriage is the form established by the association of two hordes or packs according to which the men of one group marry the women of the other;

not a marriage of individual men with individual women, but a promiscuous relationship, each man of one group marry all the women of the other group - atleast in theory - The World History Vol. 1- p.48.

31. Modesty is a derived feeling; it cannot exist until a high state of individualism has been attained, until each man desires exclusial possession of his wife and therefore wishes to shield her from covetousness of the other men. This leads to the performance of sexual intercourse private; and gradually to the concealment of the organs of sex. The World History Vol. 1-p. 27.
Few Englishmen see anything indecent in the nude in art but would be horrified at the idea of the sexes bathing together when nude. In Japan nothing unmodest is found in the fact that the sexes bathe together in a state of absolute nudity; but any representation of nude in art is considered indecent. - From nudity to Raiment - p. 5 - Hilaine Hiber.
The wearing of clothes is in no way natural to man, and that the earliest form of ornament was in the form of charms such as the cowrie shells worn in the soudon and elsewhere to by women to give them fertility - Ibrid - p. 9.
Greek Girls used also to appear naked on certain days of festivity and wrestle in public assembly promiscuously with boys of equal age with themselves - The Hindu Code p. XVIII - Dr. H.S. GOUR, D.C. LLD.
He (Lycurgus) ordered that they should go naked as well as young men, and dance too in that condition at their solemn feasts and sacrifices, singing certian songs, whilst the people stood in a ring about them, seeing and hearing them. The Kings and the whole senate as well as the rested of the City went to see and hear all that passed - Amatory Curiose - p. 38.
32. Among Anglosaxons usually a widow was put to death on her deceased husband’s pyre or at his grave side. If she did not offer herself her death she was scorned and hated - Sex and culture - p. 399. J.D.Uwin.
33. The male argus pheasant, noted for the extreme beauty of the plumage, shows himself off before the female by erecting his tail and expanding his magnificiant
wing feathers....The bullfinch makes his advances in point of the female and then puffs out his breast so that many more of the crimson feathers are seen than would otherwise be the case. The males of certain baboon species are said to turn their coloured surface to the females - Darwin.
34. It is further said that their newly married girls are even compelled to co-habit with their huaband‘s near relatives. Married women are allowed to bestow their favours upon their husband’s relations, and it is an understanding that a man should not enter his dwelling, if he sees another’s slippers placed outside as a sign that the owner of them is with the mistress of the house-Castes and Tribes of Southern India Vol.7 - p. 182.
Valians of Pattukkotai in Tanjore District say intimacy between a man and woman before marriage is tolerated, and children of such union are regarded as members of the castle and permitted to intermarry with others provided that the parties pay a nominal penalty imposed by the council - Ibid.
35. According to Judge Lindsey there are atleast fifty thousand girls in NewYork living with men who are not their husbands, and knowing both London and NewYork we are of the opinion that London can easily rival these figures. Free unions of a more or less durable nature which are really natural however will be considered later and what here concerns us is the increase in occasional semipromiscuous sexual intercourse between the ages of 18 and thirtyfive - which is effectively eliminating the necessity of commercial prostitution - Marriage- Past Present and Future.
36. Today in the City of Paris there are seventy thousand registered prostitutes, and certainly several times that number unregistered and every other city and town in the country has its large share. The houses of prostitution in France are filled with young, childless, exmarried woman, while most of the semipublic prostitutes in the country are of the same class - Thurston’s philosophy of marriage pp. 53, 54.
37. In Cyprus it appears that before marriage all women were formerly obliged by custom to prostitute themselves to strangers at the sanctuary of the goddess, where she went by the name of Aphrodite, Astarte, or what not. Similar customs prevailed in many parts of Western Asia. Whatever the motive the practice was clearly regarged not as an orgy of lust, but as a solemn religious duty performed in the service of the great mother goddess of Western Asia, whose name varied, while her type remained constant from place to place. Thus at Babylon every women whether rich or poor, had once in her lifetime to submit to the embrace of a stranger at the temple of Mylitta that is of Ishar or Astrate and to dedicate to the goddess the wages earned by the sanctified harlotry. The sacred precinct was crowded with women waiting to observe the custom. Some of them had to wait there for years at the Heliopolis or Baalbec in Syria famous for the imposing grandour of the ruined temples. The custom of the country required that every maiden should prostitute herself to a stranger at the temple of Astarte. Matrons as well as maidens testified their devotion to the Goddess in the same mannar.
In Phonecian temples women prostitute themselves for life in the service of religion, believing that by this conduct they propitiated the goddess and won her favour. It was a law of the Amorites that she who was about to marry should set in fornication seven days by the gate...At Bablus the people shaved their heads in the annual mourning for Adonis. The women who refused to sacrifice their hair had to give themselves up to strangers on a certain day of the festival and the money which they earned was devoted to the Godddess. This custom may have been migration of as older rule when at Babylus as else where formerly compelied every women with exception to sacrifice of her virtue in the service of religion. We are told that in Lydia all girls were obliged to prostitute themselves in order to earn adowry, but we may suspect that the real motive of the custom was devotion rather than economy. The suspician is confirmed by a Greek inscription found at Talles in Lydia, which proves that the practical religious prostitution survives in the country as late as second century of our era. It records of a certian woman Aurchia Aemilia by name, not only that she herself served God in the capacity of a harlot a his express command, but that her mother and other female ancestors have done the same before her. In Armania the noblest females dedicated their daughters to the service of the Goddess Anaites as prostitutes for a long time before they were given in marriage.
Nobody scrupled to take one of these girls to wife when her period of service was over. Again the Goddess Ma was served by a multitude of sacred harlots in Commanu, and crowds of men and women flocked to her sanctuary from the neighbouring cities and country to attend the biennial festivals or to pay their vows to tht Goddess-Golden Bough. Part IV.- p. 39.
Not was this Babylonian temple mentioned by Haeradotus an isolated case. The Mylatta cult was practised in many parts of the country. In other lands notably Egypt and Greece, the women served their respective duties in much the same way. Starbo says that the Temple of Aphrodite Porne at Corinth contained no fewer than a thousand harlo’s at a time all of them served the Goddess. Trather affirms this same historian. Among the ancient Greeks it was customary to dedicate to Zeus the most beautiful vergin girls available. They were forced to remain in the service of the deity giving their bodies to all men who sought them, untill the outset of mensuration proclaimed the embrace marriage, In the temple of Aesculapus near Tithcrasa, there was always in readiness a bed prepared for the purpose of incubation which was practised worship - p. 65.
38. In an old manuscript, which contains a collection of pieces in the Irish language compiled about A.D. 1100 it is said Conchobar, King of Uister, who lived at the time when Christ was born, deflowered all the virgins of his kingdom and it is indicated that the defloweration of maidens was a duty incubent upon them - A short History of Marriage - p. 15. Edward Westermark.
39. Numbudries were entitled to the first fruits; and it was considered the high privillege to every Nair maid to be introduced by them to womanhood. In this
connection reference may be made to Hamill’s new account of the East Indies where it is stated, “ when Zomarin married he must not cohabit with his bride till the Nambudiri or chief priest has enjoyed her. And he, if he pleases may have three nights of her nuptuals because the first fruits of her nuptuals must be an holy oblation to the god she worships, and some of the nobles are so complaisant as to allow the clergy the same tribute, but the common people cannot have the compliment paid to them, but are forced to supply the Priest’s place for themselves - Castes and Tribes of Southern India - Thurston.
It has been prosposed to derive the religious prostitution of Western Asia from a privilege secular and precutionary practise if destroying a bride’s virginity before handing her over in order that the bridegroom’s intercourse should be safe from a peril that is much dreaded by men in certian stage of culture Golden Bough IV p. 50 - Fraser.
40. A Fuegian husband is not even permitted to speak to his wife after her confinement unless she gives him leave to do so..... In primitive socities it is a very general rule that all cohabitation must cease when a woman became pregnancy. And the seperation between her and her husband is commonly observed during the whole time that she is nursing the child. The latter period is very much longer among uncultured peoples than with European women.....To suckle her childern for even four years; and even five, six or seven years is quite usual for a primitive woman. From two to three years is the most general duration of nursing. Throughout the general part of that time marital relation among many uncultured people cease entirely....The usage was general among the tribes of America and the restriction extended from the first recognition of pregnancy to

the end of the period of sucking - In Figi it is quite out of the question for a man to have relations with his wife at any time she is pregnant of is suckling a child that is for about four years. In New Caledonia abstention from marital relations is also observed during the whole duration of both pregnancy and nursing that is for four or five years. In Solomon Islands after the birth of the child the husband is not supposed to cohabit with his wife until child could walk. So again in Loyality islands a man may not even see his wife until the child is able to crawl - The Mothers Vol. 2 - p. 391.

piyūcit tīvilē maṉaivi karuk koṇṭirukkuṅ kālattil kaṇavaṉ avaḷuṭaṉ pēcattāṉum aṉumatikkappaṭamāṭṭāṉ. muṟkālac camūkaṅkaḷil peṇ karuk koṇṭa tum āṇ peṇ cērkkai aṟavē oḻikkappaṭavēṇṭum eṉṉum kaṭṭāyam iruntu vantatu. karu vaḷarcciyuṟṟa piṟkālattilum maṉaivi piḷḷaiyaip peṟṟup pāl koṭukkum kālattilum kaṇavaṉum maṉaiviyum pirintiruntārkaḷ. tiruntāta makkaḷiṭaiyē piḷḷaikaḷukkup pālūṭṭi vaḷarkkuṅ kālam mikavum nīṇṭatu. vaṭa amerikkariṭaiyē peṇ karukkoṇṭu piḷḷaiyaip peṟṟup pālūṭṭi vaḷarkkuṅ kālam muḻumaiyum kaṇavaṉōṭu cērntu vāḻum toṭarpu muṟṟāka nīkkappaṭṭuḷḷatu. itaṉāl nālu āṇṭukaḷ varaiyil kaṇavaṉ, maṉaiviyuṭaṉ toṭarpu vaittiruttal muṭiyātatākum. nīyukaliṭōṉiyā eṉṉum iṭattilum maṉaivi karukkoṇṭatiliruntu iruvarum kaṇavaṉ maṉaivi eṉṉum toṭarpiṉṟi nālu allatu aintu āṇṭukaḷ vāḻtalākiya vaḻakkam uṇṭu. calamaṉ tīvukaḷil piḷḷai naṭakkum paruvam aṭaivataṉ muṉ kaṇavaṉ maṉaiviyuṭaṉ cēra aṉumatikkap paṭamāṭṭāṉ. lōyāliṟṟit tīvukaḷil piḷḷai tavaḻum paruvam aṭaiyumuṉ kaṇavaṉ maṉaiviyaip pārkkattāṉum muṭiyātu.
